## Sustainability Statement

How to read this statement General disclosures Environmental information Social information Governance information Annexes

## How to read this statement

In accordance with the Corporate Sustainability Reporting Directive (CSRD), our 2024 Annual Report integrates financial and non-financial data. The non-financial data is part of this separate section, entitled Sustainability Statement.

Our Sustainability Statement is structured according to the four sections in the European Sustainability Reporting Standards (ESRS): "General disclosures", "Environmental

information", "Social information" and "Governance information". Under each of these four sections, you will find the details on the sustainability related matters that are material to OMV Petrom, in line with the results of our Double Materiality Assessment.

Below is a mapping of our material topics and the related ESRS chapter in the Sustainability Statement.

The structure of the Sustainability Statement chapter is guided by the requirements of ESRS and mostly follows the same format.

Governance, where we describe our governance processes, controls and procedures we use to monitor and manage our material impacts, risks and opportunities.

Strategy, where we describe the approach we take on the respective topic; the interaction of our material impacts, risks and opportunities with our strategy and business model; and how the interests and views of our stakeholders were taken into account when building the strategy;

Impact, risk and opportunity management is the section where we detail the policies we have in place on the respective topic as well as the actions we take in relation to our material impacts, risks and opportunities. For some social topics, you will also find information about how we engage with our stakeholders and channels where our stakeholders can raise concerns;

Metrics and targets include the key performance indicators we use to manage our material negative impacts, advancing positive impacts and managing material risks and opportunities.

At the beginning of each paragraph, you will find the coding of the respective ESRS Disclosure Requirement that it addresses.

<!-- Report Page 78 -->
## General disclosures


Starting with 2024 reporting year, the sustainability related information is included in the Sustainability Statement, section of the Annual Report, and was prepared in accordance with the European Sustainability Reporting Standards (ESRS) introduced by the European Corporate Sustainability Reporting Directive (CSRD) and transposed into the Romanian legislation under Ministry of Finance Order no. 85/2024, which also amended the Ministry of Finance Order 2844/2016. In line with ESRS requirements, OMV Petrom updated its materiality analysis in 2024. This Statement also includes disclosures required by Article 8 of the EU Taxonomy Regulation (Regulation EU 2020/852).

In relation to the preparation of this Sustainability Statement in accordance with ESRS standards, KPMG Audit SRL has performed a limited assurance engagement on the Sustainability Statement, in accordance with International Standard on Assurance Engagements (ISAE) 3000 (Revised), Assurance engagements other than audits or

reviews of historical financial information (ISAE 3000 (Revised)).

### Basis for preparation [DR BP-1] General basis for preparation of sustainability statements

[ESRS 2-BP-1.5a] This Sustainability Statement has been prepared on a consolidated basis at the OMV Petrom Group level, headquartered in Coralilor Street, no. 22, sector 1, postal code 013329, Bucharest, Romania.

[ESRS 2-BP-1.5b i] The consolidation scope of the Sustainability Statement is the same as for the financial statements, covering all consolidated entities for which OMV Petrom holds a stake of more than 50% and holds operational control. In case of Health & Safety metrics, these are presented for OMV Petrom Group as well as for OMV Petrom Group including OMV Petrom Global Solutions S.R.L. (Romanian entity accounted using equity method).

[ESRS 2-BP-1.5b ii] For details regarding the OMV Petrom Group structure, please see Note 32 from the Consolidated Financial Statements.

[ESRS 2-BP-1.5c] Within the present Sustainability Statement, OMV Petrom has also included information related to its upstream and downstream value chain, where identified as relevant as part of the Double Materiality Assessment. Relevant information related to the value chain includes material impacts, risks and opportunities that may affect or arise from upstream and downstream business relationships, policies and actions that extend over value chain and metrics, that may consolidate value chain data. Where information presented includes value chain information it shall be stated so, unless explicitly required by the data point.

[ESRS 2-BP-1.5d] OMV Petrom has not used the option to omit a specific piece of information corresponding to intellectual property, know-how or the results of innovation.

[ESRS 2-BP-1.5e] OMV Petrom has not opted to withhold information about impending developments or ongoing negotiations due to the exemption allowed in articles 19a(3) and 29a(3) of Directive 2013/34/EU.

### [DR BP-2] Disclosures in relation to specific circumstances

[ESRS 2-BP-2.9, ESRS 2-BP-2.9a] When preparing this sustainability statement, the same definitions of time horizons as prescribed by ESRS 1 paragraph 6.4 were used. As such, the short-term time horizon refers to the period adopted as the reporting period in the financial statements; the medium-term time horizon: from the end of the shortterm reporting period up to 5 years; and respectively the long-term time horizon: more than 5 years.

[ESRS 2-BP-2.10a, b, c, d]. Scope 3 GHG emissions - encompasses indirect emissions that occur in the upstream value chain, which are reported under Category 3.1 Purchased good and services, as well as in the downstream value chain, namely the emissions reported under categories 3.10 Processing of sold products and 3.11 Use of sold products. This data is estimated using indirect sources.

[ESRS 2-BP-2.11a, b] In 2024, all disclosed data related to quantitative metrics and monetary amounts were measured.

[ESRS 2-BP-2.13a, b, c] Where changes in the preparation and presentation of sustainability information occurred compared to the previous reporting period, such changes are disclosed alongside the respective metrics.

<!-- Report Page 79 -->
[ESRS 2-BP-2.15] For entity-specific topics, OMV Petrom has considered the application requirements (AR) provided by the ESRS, while also relying on GRI standards for disclosures. The references to the Taskforce on ClimateRelated Financial Disclosures (TCFD) were discontinued, due to the overlap of these disclosures with the European Sustainability Reporting Standards. For the Double Materiality Assessment, SASB and GRI were used as a reference to identify potentially relevant topics. This Sustainability Statement also serves as our Communication on Progress for the United Nations Global Compact (UNGC).

### Governance

#### [DR GOV-1] The role of the administrative, management and supervisory bodies

[ESRS 2-GOV-1.21a] OMV Petrom is governed in a two-tier system, with the Executive Board managing the daily business and operations of the Company, under the supervision of the Supervisory Board.

The Supervisory Board is made up of nine non-executive members appointed by the General Meeting of Shareholders, with the exception of interim members, who are appointed by the Supervisory Board itself. One of the Supervisory Board members serves as President, after being appointed by the Supervisory Board. The current Supervisory Board tenure is until April 2025.

The Supervisory Board appoints the Executive Board for a four-year term. Currently, the Executive Board is comprised of five members whose tenure is until April 2027.

[ESRS 2-GOV-1.21b] In accordance with the provisions of the collective bargaining agreement and the applicable laws, the Trade Union representatives are invited to attend the meetings of the Supervisory Board, as well as of the Executive Board when collective lay-off projects are approved and can raise matters regarding the rights and interests of the employees.

[ESRS 2-GOV-1.21c] All Supervisory Board members have wide experience in the sectors that are relevant for the company, from energy to mobility and chemicals, working for various companies directly in the core business or in finance, legal or consulting for the sector. Their experience covers a wide array of geographies, with a focus on the

Central and Eastern Europe (CEE) region and, for part of the members, on Romania. For more information on their professional qualifications, please also see the relevant section in the Corporate Governance Report.

The five members of the Executive Board of OMV Petrom have a vast experience in the energy sector. Two of them have worked across several geographies, while the other three have worked mainly in Romania or the CEE area. All Executive Board members have a deep understanding of the local markets in which the company is active. For more information on their professional qualifications, please also see the relevant section in the Corporate Governance Report.

<!-- Report Page 82 -->

For more information on the independence of Supervisory Board members please also see the relevant section in the Corporate Governance Report.

[ESRS 2-GOV-1.22a] The bodies that are responsible for oversight over the company's impacts, risks and opportunities are:

The Executive Board, through specific task-forces which include all Executive Board members and internal

subject matter experts relevant for the sustainability matters discussed in regular meetings.

The Supervisory Board, through the Audit Committee, with a focus on the EWRM (Enterprise-Wide Risk Management) risks.

[ESRS 2-GOV-1.22b] The Internal Rules for the Executive Board state that the Executive Board shall provide for a risk management system corresponding with OMV Petrom Group's requirements. The potential impacts and risks can be linked with the risks assessed across the organization according to the enterprise-wide risk management process. The Executive Board also approves the company's strategy

and objectives, the annual risk management strategy, as well as the hedging measures in the Group. The Executive Board reports to the Supervisory Board regularly, in a timely manner and comprehensively, all relevant issues of business development, including the risk situation and the risk management of the Company.

The responsibilities of the Audit Committee, as a body of the Supervisory Board, as per its Terms of Reference, are, amongst others, to:

undertake an annual assessment of the system of internal control, while the assessment should consider the effectiveness and scope of the internal audit function, the adequacy of risk management and internal control reports to the Audit Committee, management's responsiveness and effectiveness in dealing with identified internal control failings or weaknesses and the submission of relevant reports to the Supervisory Board and

evaluate the efficiency of the internal control system and risk management system.

[ESRS 2-GOV-1.22c i] The two-tier system of management allows the company to assess all material economic, social, environmental and governance impacts, risks and opportunities.

The Executive Board manages the organization's impact on the economy, environment, and society. This responsibility

includes supervision over all material topics outlined, such as climate change, human rights, and safety, among others.

The Executive Board determines the development strategy and policies, ensures adherence to corporate governance principles within the Group and provides regular updates to the Supervisory Board on all important business matters, including strategy implementation, company risk profile, and risk management. Matters related to sustainability, including key material aspects like Health, Safety, Security, and Environment (HSSE), climate change, and business ethics, are discussed in Executive Board meetings and integrated into the Company's daily operations and management processes.

Sustainability topics are on the agenda of regular Executive Board meetings, as well as of OMV Petrom's dedicated taskforces. The Executive Board manages, monitors and oversees impacts, risks and opportunities by approving the Sustainability Statement. The Supervisory Board exercises its oversight role by approving the Sustainability Statement.

The Sustainability sub-department is part of the Communication and Sustainability Department that reports directly to the CEO and it is responsible for the integration, coordination, and publishing of the Sustainability Statement. See below a graphical representation of the reporting lines to the administrative, management and supervisory bodies, in relation to sustainability matters.

The Audit Committee oversees the main risks and mitigation measures and evaluates the efficiency of the internal control and risk management systems, validating their adequacy. The Audit Committee reviews and makes recommendations to the Supervisory Board in relation to matters such as financial reporting, external auditing, internal auditing, internal controls, risk management, as well as compliance, conduct and conflicts of interest. The Audit Committee oversees, among others, the following sustainability areas: Climate change, Affected communities and Own workforce.

[ESRS 2-GOV-1.22c ii] The subject matter experts within OMV Petrom monitor the implementation of policies, actions, targets and the progress on metrics, in relation to material impacts, risks and opportunities. They report to the heads of functions who own the material topics, which in turn report to the respective Executive Board members.

[ESRS 2-GOV-1.22c iii] Management of impacts, risks and opportunities is covered by policies in place, described in the topical chapters of the Sustainability Statement, related to environmental, social and governance topics. Such policies, that include a broad set of internal regulations, are monitored through the internal control system available at OMV Petrom Group level.

Moreover, through the existing taskforces, progress on impacts, risks and opportunities is monitored by the Executive Board. More details on roles and responsibilities of the Executive Board are included in the relevant section of the Corporate Governance Report.

[ESRS 2-GOV-1.22d] Accountability for the OMV Petrom Sustainability Framework and its targets resides with the Executive Board. Specific functions are nominated as responsible for setting and implementing OMV Petrom's short- and medium-term sustainability targets within various task-forces. The setting and monitoring of targets is performed regularly through the special task-forces and regular Executive Board meetings. The Supervisory Board oversees the setting of climate related targets and progress towards them within their meetings. For all other sustainability matters, the Supervisory Board oversees the target achievement as part of the Sustainability Statement approval process.

[ESRS 2-GOV-1.23] All sustainability-related matters are managed by experts in their respective fields, from HSSE to People & Culture or Compliance. These internal subject matter experts and the managers of the functional teams responsible for the respective sustainability topic are part of the special task-forces mentioned above, which give oversight to the Executive Board on sustainability matters.

In cases where the Executive Board needs to gain a deeper understanding of the required skills, assessments are performed either internally or with external support. For instance, the Low carbon business capabilities assessment program was undertaken to identify the skills necessary for the company to be prepared for new lines of business supporting the energy transition.

[ESRS 2-GOV-1.23b] The skills and experience available in the company are related to sustainability impacts, risks and opportunities through the actual qualifications and expertise of the people involved in proposing and reviewing policies, actions and targets.

<!-- Report Page 83 -->
### [DR GOV-1 G1 ] Business conduct matters

[G1.GOV-1.5a] Business conduct topics are regularly included in discussions at Supervisory Board level, in Audit Committee meetings, ensuring ethical considerations are a constant focus in financial and operational oversight. This also ensures access to expertise on business conduct matters.

The Executive Board formally supports the Code of Business Ethics, which outlines a strict zero-tolerance stance on bribery, fraud, theft, and other corrupt activities.

[G1.GOV-1.5b] Members of the Executive Board receive continuous education and updates on business ethics, managed by the Compliance Officer through quarterly Executive Board reporting updates. Such reports may refer to policy developments in relation to business ethics, international sanctions, whistleblowing reports, various external consultant reports on matters related to business ethics, and other topics. Access to expertise related to business ethics is ensured through the Compliance Officer who reports directly to the CEO of OMV Petrom.

Additionally, every three years, the Executive Board members receive business ethics training. The training is facilitated directly by the Compliance Officer. The Compliance Officer can also attend Audit Committee meetings.

#### [DR GOV-2] Information provided to and sustainability matters addressed by the undertaking's administrative, management and supervisory bodies

[ESRS 2-GOV-2.26a] The Executive Board of OMV Petrom manages the organization's impact on the economy, environment, and society. This responsibility includes supervision over all material topics outlined in this Sustainability Statement.

The Executive Board of OMV Petrom is informed by the material topic owners and subject matter experts about the material impacts, risks and opportunities and the implementation of due diligence, and the results and effectiveness of policies, actions, metrics and targets adopted through its regular weekly meetings, as well as its task-forces. These task-forces are organized with varying frequencies depending on the topic, ranging from bi-monthly to quarterly or to every semester.

In 2024, the Executive Board was informed about all material impacts, risks and opportunities resulted from the Double Materiality Assessment in a stakeholder's management task-force meeting in Q3. Also, throughout 2024, the CEO and CFO had regular meetings with the internal subject matter experts to discuss material impacts, risks and opportunities, as well the policies, actions and targets to mitigate or advance them.

The Executive Board provides regular updates to the Supervisory Board on various business matters, including strategy implementation, actions, metrics and targets related to sustainability topics. In 2024, these topics included updates on Low Carbon business development, methane emissions and GHG targets.

[ESRS 2-GOV-2.26b] When making decisions on major transactions, the Executive Board of OMV Petrom considers a series of criteria: business strategic targets, financial metrics, risk profile, and climate targets impact. This set of criteria assists the Executive Board in objectively identifying and reviewing OMV Petrom's projects, enabling a comprehensive portfolio optimization across the Group in support of its strategic goals, including greenhouse gas emissions reduction pathway. The same criteria are applied when major decisions are submitted for approval to the Supervisory Board.

At OMV Petrom, we have also internally defined Sustainability CapEx as investments that meet either of two criteria: alignment with the EU Taxonomy Regulation or investments that assist in implementing OMV Petrom's 2030 Sustainability Framework.

[ESRS 2-GOV-2.26c] All impacts, risks and opportunities related to own operations, as listed in paragraph SBM-3.48 of this chapter, were addressed at Executive Board level in their various regular meetings or in task-forces throughout the year 2024.

All climate change related impacts, risks and opportunities are discussed during Supervisory Board meetings.

In 2024, all impacts, risks and opportunities related to Climate change, Affected communities and Own workforce were discussed in the Audit Committee, which is a consultative committee at Supervisory Board level.

[DR GOV-3], [DR GOV-3 E1] Integration of sustainabilityrelated performance in incentive schemes

[ESRS 2-GOV-3.29a] The Executive Board remuneration at OMV Petrom consists of fixed and variable remuneration elements as well as benefits. The variable remuneration, which includes a Long-Term Incentive Plan (LTIP) and annual bonus, is aligned with the Strategy 2030 and places a strong emphasis on sustainability performance, including Environment, Social, Governance (ESG) related key performance indicators, such as carbon emission reduction.

For more information regarding the Remuneration Policy, please see the Corporate Governance Report under the Report of the governing bodies chapter.

[ESRS 2-GOV-3.29b] The Long-Term Incentive Plan (LTIP) focuses on sustainability targets and links the remuneration of the Executive Board members also to the achievement of the greenhouse gas emissions reduction target of OMV Petrom.

[ESRS 2-GOV-3.29c, ESRS 2-GOV-3.29d, E1-GOV-3.13] In LTIP, the GHG emissions reduction target of OMV Petrom has a weight of 25%. Increase of the proportion of women at management level has a weight of 10%.

Various Safety KPIs, including Zero-work related fatalities and Process Safety Event Rate and Total Recordable Injury Rate are included in the 2024 annual bonus scheme with a cumulated 15% weighting.

[ESRS 2-GOV-3.29e] The Remuneration Policy in place applies to the Executive Board and Supervisory Board since
<!-- Report Page 84 -->
2021. The Supervisory Board remuneration is fixed.
Following the launch of the Strategy 2030, an updated Remuneration Policy was prepared by the Presidential and Nomination Committee and approved by the Ordinary General Meeting of Shareholders in April 2022, with a majority of 92% of votes cast.

The definition of the climate-related consideration is based on the reduction of direct GHG emissions (Scope 1) and indirect GHG emissions from energy purchased (Scope 2) from operated and/or majority owned assets in 2024 against 2019.

### [DR GOV-5] Risk management and internal controls over sustainability reporting

[ESRS 2-GOV-5.36a] The main features and components of the risk management and internal control systems in relation to sustainability reporting follow the OMV Petrom's Internal Control framework section, as described in the Director's Report, part of the Report of the governing bodies chapter.

In scope of risk management and internal controls in relation to sustainability reporting process are the environmental, social and governance areas.

[ESRS 2-GOV-5.36b] Our risk assessment and internal control systems are designed to identify, assess, and mitigate risks that could affect our financial and sustainability reporting. We perform periodic reviews for each end-to-end process defined in the risk and internal controls matrixes at least every three years, or sooner if triggered by internal/external factors. This approach enables us to detect emerging risks and update the existing ones, if needed,

prioritizing risks based on materiality, process complexity, and likelihood of errors.

OMV Petrom's internal control framework encompasses policies, procedures, and controls that are reviewed periodically and updated to address risks and comply with regulatory requirements.

[ESRS 2-GOV-5.36c] Potential risks related to the sustainability reporting process refer mainly to completeness and accuracy of data used. In line with Internal Control System Directive, mitigation strategies are established, which consist in various control activities.

The Sustainability sub-department, together with support functions responsible for the content, perform various data checks, in order to ensure completeness and integrity of the data.

[ESRS 2-GOV-5.36d] Monitoring activities are ongoing, as part of OMV Petrom's internal control system framework.

The findings of such activities are assessed and communicated, and further mitigated through remediation actions, together with responsible internal functions.

[ESRS 2-GOV-5.36e] The Executive Board of OMV Petrom is responsible to establish an internal control system, including independent assurance functions. Internal audit function evaluates the effectiveness of the internal control system, during audits, based on the internal audit plan, and provides recommendation for improvements based on identified deficiencies.

The Audit Committee of the Supervisory Board evaluates the efficiency of OMV Petrom internal control system. Details on the main duties and powers of the Audit Committee, including its functioning, are described in the relevant section of the Corporate Governance Report.

A yearly presentation to the Executive Board and to the Audit Committee of the Supervisory Board is prepared, focused on the general framework, the execution of the annual work program and priorities for the next year, in relation to the internal controls system.

## Strategy

<!-- Report Page 86 -->
### [DR SBM-1] Strategy, business model and value chain

[ESRS 2-SBM-1.40a i, ESRS 2-SBM-1.40a ii] OMV Petrom's ambition is to lead the energy transition in Romania and South-East Europe, capitalizing on emerging markets opportunities, while delivering value for all its stakeholders. Our goal is to become a partner of choice for all the customer groups we serve: from retail customers of our filling stations, to gas and power wholesale customers represented by small, medium and large businesses in various industries, such as construction, aviation or oil companies.

Our Strategy 2030 sets out the path we plan to take to reach our ambitions, through three key pillars, which refer to the products and services we offer as well as the markets we serve.

### Transition to low and zero carbon

A greener and more diversified portfolio will be reached by investing in renewable power generation, biofuels, alternative mobility and by assessing the potential for other new technologies, such as carbon capture and storage (CCS), energy storage, geothermal energy, and biogas. All these will pave the way to achieving Net Zero operations (Scope 1 and Scope 2 emissions) by 2050 and will support our customers to decarbonize. Approximately EUR 3.7 bn is to be invested between 2022 and 2030 to ensure cleaner

energy. These investments enable us to achieve the following by 2030:

The largest new solar and wind power portfolio in Romania: installed capacity of ~2.5 GW, of which 60% solar and 40% wind.

The first major producer of sustainable fuels in SE Europe: 300 kt/year production of biofuels supported by the 15 kt production of green hydrogen.

A consolidated position as 1st choice mobility provider in Romania: more than 5,000 electric charging points (own and in partnership) to be installed in the operating region.

Unique opportunities, in addition to renewables, sustainable fuels, e-mobility, such as: carbon capture and storage (CCS), energy storage, geothermal, biogas.

### Grow Regional Gas

Natural gas is a key enabler for decarbonization. In this regard, Neptun Deep is a strategic project for OMV Petrom, for Romania and for the region, with plans to develop 100bcm of natural gas. The extensive knowledge acquired in more than 40 years of offshore operations can help unlock potential for further growth beyond Romania, in the Black Sea region. In terms of our product portfolio, we will transform the oil-gas mix in our E&P portfolio, with gas expected to reach 70% in 2030.

### Optimize traditional business

While demand for green energy grows, traditional energy is expected to stay in demand due to the gradual nature of transition to greener energy. Therefore, OMV Petrom will continue to modernize and further integrate its current assets, to ensure security of energy supply and generate value.

For more information regarding our Strategy 2030: Transforming for a lower carbon future, please see the OMV Petrom Strategy 2030 chapter.

[ESRS 2-SBM-1.40a iii] Our own workforce plays a crucial role in the successful delivery and support of our company's strategy. Please see information on the headcount of employees by region in the Own Workforce chapter, DR S1- 6 Characteristics of the undertaking's employees.

[ESRS 2-SBM-1.40a iv] OMV Petrom does not have any products and services that are banned in certain markets.

[ESRS 2-SBM-1.40d i] OMV Petrom is active in oil and gas exploration and production, processing, refining and marketing of refined petroleum products, as well as in power generation and marketing of gas and electricity. In 2024, the revenues derived from fossil-fuel related activities are

disclosed in note 19 "Sales revenues", line "Total sales revenues" to the consolidated financial statements.

[ESRS 2-SBM-1.40d ii, iii, iv] OMV Petrom' activities do not fall under division 20.2 regarding Manufacture of pesticides and other agrochemical products as per Annex I to Regulation (EC) no. 1893/2006. Also, the company is not active in controversial weapons (anti-personnel mines, cluster munitions, chemical weapons and biological weapons), nor in the cultivation and production of tobacco.

[ESRS 2-SBM-1.40e, f] OMV Petrom aims to transform its portfolio of products and services in order to keep supplying the energy needs for today, while transitioning towards low and zero carbon. We have set targets for reducing the carbon footprint across all scopes and reaching carbon neutral operations (Scope 1 and Scope 2) by 2050.

The planned sustainability-related business portfolio goals are outlined in sections ESRS 2-SBM-1.40a i and ii.

Our sustainability-related goals address both internal and external stakeholders and they are reflected in our 2030 targets.

<!-- Report Page 87 -->
For targets and progress related to:

Health and safety, training and development, human rights and level of engagement for own workforce, please see Own Workforce and Business conduct chapters.

Health and safety for workers in the value chain and ESG screening of new suppliers, please see Workers in the value chain chapter.

Yearly spend with local suppliers and affected communities' coverage by social investments, please see Affected communities chapter.

Carbon footprint reduction, please see Climate change chapter.

For details regarding the assessment of OMV Petrom's products, markets and customer groups as of end of 2024, please see the Strategy 2030 section of the Annual Report.

[ESRS 2-SBM-1.40g] Our Sustainability framework is fully embedded in the 2030 Business Strategy, with a focus on transition to low carbon business, running responsible operations, fostering people & communities and innovation & digitalization.

Through 2030, we will continue to have as priority the health and safety of our employees, customers and partners.

As part of our 2030 commitments, we plan to assemble and maintain a skilled, diverse and committed team, while persistently creating a positive influence in our communities. OMV Petrom stands by the basic principles of a just transition, concentrating on social dialogue, protection, and retention, along with re-skilling or up-skilling of employees. The firm is devoted to fostering a diverse workforce and an inclusive work culture, which is considered critical to its success.

We endorse the development of technology, research, and innovative practices to facilitate broader incorporation of technologies that will help us realize our business and climate objectives. CCS has a key role to play in decarbonization, however the pace imposed by recently approved regulations at EU level is challenging. While we are working with our stakeholders to make CCS projects commercially viable, the development of CCS projects requires high investments, fit for purpose regulatory framework and public acceptance.

[ESRS 2-SBM-1.42, ESRS 2-SBM-1.42a] This section covers the key aspects of our business model and value chain included in the Double Materiality Assessment for the current reporting year.

OMV Petrom is the largest integrated energy producer in Southeastern Europe. The company is active along the entire energy value chain: from exploration and production of oil and gas, to refining and marketing of refined petroleum products, and further on to power generation and marketing of gas and power. The company is organized into three operationally integrated business segments Exploration & Production (E&P), Refining & Marketing (R&M) and Gas & Power (G&P).

Our company has a leading position in the fuels and natural gas markets in Romania and an important contribution to the country's security of electricity supply.

In E&P, OMV Petrom is present in Romania and Bulgaria. In Romania, OMV Petrom's E&P activities are centered around six local assets, operating 149 commercial oil and gas fields. These include both onshore and shallow offshore exploration and production, as well as deep offshore exploration. By the end of 2024, our portfolio included 395 million boe in proved oil and gas reserves. Annual hydrocarbon production reached 39.91 million boe, which included 2.65 million tons of crude oil and natural gas liquids and 3.18 billion cubic meters of natural gas. In the Han Asparuh block, Bulgaria, we signed an agreement to transfer a 50% interest, while maintaining our role as operator, and continue exploration activity.

In R&M, OMV Petrom operates the Petrobrazi refinery, which has a capacity of 4.5 mn tons per year and primarily processes domestically produced crude oil in order to obtain petroleum products, thus maximizing the company's integration value. Additionally, the Company continuously evaluates the economic benefits from processing imported crude oil. During 2024, around 35% of crude oil processed by OMV Petrom was imported.

We are present on the oil products retail market through a network of 778 filling stations located in Romania, Moldova Republic, Bulgaria, and Serbia. In some of our filling stations in Romania, Bulgaria and Serbia we complemented the offer to our customers by installing charging points for electric vehicles (end-2024: ~ 900 charging points, own and in partnerships).

In Gas & Power (G&P), we are engaged in electricity production, as well as gas and power sales. In Romania, we operate the Brazi gas fired power plant, which has a capacity of 860 MW. We carry out gas and power marketing operations in seven countries, while expanding market access in Romania's geographical proximity.

OMV Petrom's sales to third parties are split by geographical areas, Romania and Central and Eastern Europe represent the Group's most important geographical markets. 83% of the Group's total sales were in Romania in 2024, while the remaining 17% sales were registered in the rest of Central and Eastern Europe.

[ESRS 2-SBM-1.42b] OMV Petrom is present on relevant markets as a producer and supplier of crude oil and natural gas, petroleum products and electricity, thus contributing to energy security. Our activities strengthen Romania's and Southeastern Europe's security of supply, as well as the EU's energy resilience.

Crude oil: OMV Petrom accounts for almost the entire oil production in Romania.

Natural gas: OMV Petrom accounts for around a third of the gas production in Romania. Gas equity volumes are complemented with third party supply gas volumes, and are placed on the market, to industrial end-users, as well as to wholesalers, while also delivered to internal consumers (such as the Brazi power plant). In compliance with the legislation in force, OMV Petrom has been supplying volumes to the regulated market for households and district heating consumption since 2022 from its equity production.

Petroleum products: These products are either produced in the Petrobrazi refinery or supplied from third parties and are sold to both Romanian and international

markets. In 2024, our refined product sales accounted for approximately one third of Romania's demand.

Electricity: In 2024, our Brazi power plant covered around 10% of the national power generation mix. For the full year, Brazi power plant net electrical output reached 4.9 TWh.

We are part of the Romanian social and economic fabric, as a major investor, employer and contributor to the state budget and also through our involvement in social causes that are relevant for the country, as mentioned in the Affected communities chapter. We will continue to invest to capture market opportunities, generating value for all stakeholders.

Our proposition to shareholders is clear: growth in investment, increase in profitability and dividends - all while securing a sustainable long-term business through our energy transition.

For more details regarding benefits for stakeholders, please see the Own workforce, Affected communities and Economic value chapters.

[ESRS 2-SBM-1.42c] Our value chain encompasses suppliers and contractors, forming the upstream value chain; owned assets and own workforce, forming own operations and partners and wholesale customers, forming the downstream value chain.

Upstream value chain: For the E&P segment, supplies and contracted services largely relate to operations such as exploration, drilling of new wells and production of hydrocarbons, including maintenance and workovers and abandonment jobs. In the G&P segment, energy and trading companies account for majority of the spend. For the R&M segment, purchases include conventional feedstocks and non-fuel business products.

Own operations: More information on our own operations can be found in this chapter, under paragraph ESRS 2- SBM-1.42, which includes a description of our business segments and products.

Downstream value chain: All the sales volumes of oil and gas from E&P are distributed to own operations in R&M and G&P segments. External customers of G&P and R&M range from energy companies and wholesalers to industry and, indirectly, through our retail partners, to end customers.

[ESRS 2-SBM-2.45a v] We use the outcome of our stakeholder engagements to inform and adapt our decision-making processes, to adjust our plans, or to analyse and mitigate or advance our impacts, risks and opportunities.

[ESRS 2-SBM-2.45b] As part of OMV Petrom's due diligence processes, engagement with stakeholders occurs on a regular basis.

During the Double Materiality Assessment process for the 2024 Sustainability Statement, we engaged with various stakeholder groups, both internal and external, such as employees, customers, suppliers/contractors/business partners, capital market participants, NGOs, authorities, communities, associations/networks, media, and education, science and research organizations. The purpose of this engagement was to inform the impact assessment process. The input was used for validation and to potentially review the internal impact assessment.

[ESRS 2-SBM-2.45c i, ESRS 2-SBM-2.45d] In 2024, OMV Petrom revised its low carbon business targets and the Strategy 2030 plans, and as such the company updated the capital markets both on the revisions, as well as on the progress of Strategy 2030 implementation. OMV Petrom's strategy update was well received by major external stakeholders such as capital markets, media and business partners. To address investor's feedback, in 2024 we updated our dividend policy and revised up the minimum internal rates of return for low and zero carbon business projects, also reflecting the feedback received.

The Executive Board of OMV Petrom is informed about the views and interests of affected stakeholders as part of the approval process of the Double Materiality Assessment. The Executive Board members are also constantly being informed on the views and interests of our stakeholders through the results of the Pulse Check and direct engagement with employees and through direct meetings with representatives of investors, media, business partners or academia. The Executive Board is informed about the views and perspectives of stakeholders also by company experts who are directly in touch with these stakeholders.

Informing Supervisory Board members takes place via the Executive Board.

[ESRS 2-SBM-2.45c ii] No further steps regarding amendments to the strategy or business model to address the interests and views of stakeholders, are to be communicated at this time.

[ESRS 2-SBM-2.45c iii] We do not foresee a change in the relationship with and views of stakeholders.

<!-- Report Page 90 -->
### [DR SBM-2 S1] Interests and views of Own Workforce

[SBM-2 S1.12] OMV Petrom's commitment is to explicitly integrate human rights in its engagement with affected stakeholders - own employees, workers in the value chain and communities. Respect for human rights is a core principle in all our business activities and relations, as also reflected in OMV Petrom's Code of Conduct and Human Rights Policy Statement. These policies also affirm the values and rules the company acts by, in line with the United Nations Guiding Principles on Business and Human Rights.

OMV Petrom's own workforce is a key group of affected stakeholders. Through the Pulse Checks, regular engagements with the trade unions, as well as direct engagement with our workforce (through Executive Board webcasts, People & Culture roadshows and events, etc.), we ensure that the interests, views and rights of our people are taken into account when developing our People Strategy. Our people strategy supports the delivery of our Strategy 2030 and contributes to the adaptation of our business model.

### [DR SBM-2 S2] Interests and views of Workers in the value chain

[SBM-2 S2.9] Value chain workers represent a key group of affected stakeholders with which OMV Petrom continuously engages, as reported in the Workers in the value chain chapter. Respect for human rights of value chain workers is a core principle that is inscribed in our Code of Conduct. We have not yet identified a need to adjust our strategy or business model as a result of the engagements conducted with this category of stakeholders as described in the Workers in the Value Chain chapter.

### [DR SBM-2 S3] Interests and views of Affected communities

[SBM-2 S3.7] One of the key pillars of OMV Petrom's Sustainability Framework is "fostering people and

communities", through which the company recognizes the importance it places on the role of its local communities.

OMV Petrom's commitment is to explicitly integrate human rights in its work, in the community at large as well as sharing these values in the company's value chain. The standards set by the company are incorporated in the OMV Petrom Group Human Rights Policy Statement, that also affirms the values and rules the company acts by, in line with

the United Nations Guiding Principles on Business and Human Rights.

We have not yet identified a need to adjust our strategy or business model as a result of the engagements conducted with this category of stakeholders as described in the Affected communities chapter.

[ESRS 2-SBM-3.48c i, ii, iv] For all details regarding our impacts, risks and opportunities, please see the topical chapters.

[ESRS 2-SBM-3.48b, d] The material risks and opportunities did not have any material, measurable impact on OMV Petrom Group's current financial position, financial performance, and cash flows. There are no foreseen current

financial effects of material risks and opportunities for which there is a significant risk of a material adjustment within the next annual reporting period to the carrying amounts of assets, considering the base case scenario which is used for mid-term planning as disclosed in the Consolidated Financial Statements, Note 2 - Effects of climate change and energy transition.

[ESRS 2-SBM-3.48f] The resilience of OMV Petrom's strategy and business model is crucial in addressing material impacts and risks, while seizing opportunities. OMV Petrom's strategic framework is designed to be adaptable, enabling the Company to navigate the dynamic market environment and energy transition. We have identified climate change as a strategic risk and it is annually reviewed as part of strategic risk management process. More details can be found in the Strategic Risks section in the Director's Report, part of the Report of the governing bodies chapter. In addition, our Company's resilience is sustained by investments in renewable energy and technological innovation, positioning us to capitalize on emerging opportunities in the green energy sector. More details on 2030 GHG emissions targets to mitigate impacts and advance opportunities related to energy transition, can be found in Strategy 2030 chapter and in the Climate change chapter of the Sustainability Statement section. In the longer-term, the Company's ambition to achieve net zero operations by 2050 (Scope 1 and Scope 2) emissions highlights its long-term vision and dedication to environmental stewardship.

[ESRS 2-SBM-3.48h] Refer to the topical chapters Climate change, Pollution, Water, Biodiversity and ecosystems, Own workforce, Workers in the value chain, Affected communities and Business conduct for the lists of impacts, risks and opportunities covered by ESRS Disclosure Requirements.

Other impacts, risks and opportunities which are not covered by the ESRS Disclosure Requirements, are entity specific and they are included in the following chapters: Process safety, Economic value and Cybersecurity.

<!-- Report Page 95 -->
### Impact, risk and opportunity management

### [DR IRO-1] Description of the processes to identify and assess mateial impacts, risks and opportunities

[ESRS 2-IRO-1.53a] The Double Materiality Assessment process was carried out taking into consideration all fully consolidated entities where OMV Petrom is the operator and/or where OMV Petrom has a stake of more than 50% and exerts controlling influence.

The Double Materiality Assessment was conducted in five steps, which considered the impacts, risks, and opportunities, as well as stakeholder expectations applying the ESRS methodology of Double Materiality Assessment (DMA).

Step 1 consisted of the development of a long list of all topics included in the ESRS and other topics from relevant guidelines. The relevance of each topic was analysed by the

subject matter experts considering the nature of activities, business relationships, geographies or other factors related to OMV Petrom. The short-listed topics were further considered in the following steps.

Step 2 consisted of interviews with internal subject matter experts to identify and formulate the impacts, risks, and opportunities for the different topics.

In step 3, we conducted the stakeholder engagement, in which ten stakeholder groups (internal and external) were consulted to determine sustainability topics that are of highest relevance to them. The results were used to validate and inform the internal subject matter experts' assessment. The following groups of stakeholders were consulted: employees, customers, suppliers/contractors/business partners, capital market participants, NGOs, authorities, communities, associations/networks, media, and education, science and research organizations.

Step 4 consisted of the assessment and scoring of the impacts, risks, and opportunities identified across sustainability topics to further determine the Impact materiality and Financial materiality. The assessment was undertaken by subject matter experts, in dedicated workshops.

In step 5, the prioritization of topics was made by using a threshold previously defined and by taking into consideration the results of the previous steps.

[ESRS 2-IRO-1.53b i-iv] The assessment of impacts as part of our Double Materiality Assessment followed the 5-step process described above, in compliance with disclosure requirement ESRS 2-IRO-1.53a.

OMV Petrom leveraged its subject matter experts' experience and knowledge of the topics to make sure the focus is placed on the company's specific activities, business relationships and geographies that may give rise to heightened risk of adverse impacts.

Consultation with affected stakeholders was included to validate and inform the process performed by internal experts.

The impacts were assessed throughout the company's own operations and as a result of our business relationships, against the following set of criteria: Scale (level of seriousness of an impact), Scope (how wide-spread the impact is), Irremediability (whether and to what extent the negative impacts can be remediated, restoring environment or affected people to their prior state) and Likelihood (for

potential impacts). The evaluation took place based on the predominant positive or negative impact and various time horizons.

We applied a threshold of 8 for environmental topics, and 5 for social and governance topics.
<!-- Report Page 96 -->

[ESRS 2-IRO-1.53c i, ESRS 2-IRO-1.53c ii, ESRS 2-IRO1.53c iii, ESRS 2-IRO-1.53e, ESRS 2-IRO-1.53f] The assessment of risks and opportunities as part of our Double Materiality Assessment followed the 5-step process described above.

The risks and opportunities assessed refer to the possible future events that could adversely affect or positively enhance the Group's objectives for various time horizons as defined in ESRS 2-BP-2.9a. Risks and opportunities stemming from dependencies on natural, human and social resources were considered, such as "regulations related to emissions, energy efficiency, and increasing share of renewables in the energy mix, that might result in a decrease of fossil fuels production with loss of sales/revenue".

The existing risk management principles were applied in the impacts, risks and opportunities assessment process, as part of the Double Materiality Assessment. The potential impacts and risks can be linked with the risks assessed across the organization according to EWRM process. Sustainability-related risks can appear in various forms within the organization and intersect with different risk management activities across the Company. Prioritization of such risks is supported by step 5 described in ESRS 2-IRO1.53a. For details on EWRM process, please see Risk Management section in the Director's Report, part of the Report the Governing Bodies chapter. Opportunities are part of strategic planning, operational reviews, and investment decisions processes.

The risks and opportunities were assessed against the following set of criteria: magnitude of financial effect (how severe a financial effect is to be, on a scale of 0-none to 3- high) and likelihood of occurrence (how likely a risk or opportunity is to occur, on a scale from 0-never to 5- guaranteed).

The financial materiality threshold was defined at 1.5, thus setting high and upper range of medium financial effects as material.

[ESRS 2-IRO-1.53d] The Double Materiality Assessment process was coordinated by the Corporate Sustainability sub-department, while the internal subject matter experts

were responsible for providing input and assessment in relation to their respective topic. Senior management has been involved throughout the process by their subject matter ex-perts, who provided consultation and pre-alignment.

The results were shared for feedback with the senior management and internal subject matter experts in Q4/23. The final results referring to the material topics and subtopics that include material impacts, risks and opportunities, were presented to and approved by the Executive Board through a motion in December 2023. The results of the Double Materiality Assessment were thoroughly reviewed in 2024 by internal subject matter experts, with a more granular view on impact, risks and opportunities connected to the material topics. No material changes were identified at topic level. The results were presented to the Executive Board.

The sustainability reporting process, informed by the results of the Double Materiality Assessment, is guided by the Sustainability Directive. The Directive as well as the Process are part of the overall management process of OMV Petrom. The Sustainability Statement, as part of the OMV Petrom Annual Report, is approved by the OMV Petrom Executive Board and Supervisory Board, as well as subject to approval and presented to shareholders in the Annual General Shareholders Meeting.

[ESRS 2-IRO-1.53g] For the Double Materiality Assessment, OMV Petrom has used available information from internal reports and operational data. The information was sourced mainly from existing reporting on ESG topics and workshops with internal subject matter experts dealing with ESG management. Additionally, sector/industry standards have been reviewed to inform the double materiality process regarding sector-specific impacts (GRI, SASB etc). External and internal stakeholders were also included in the process. Each value chain segment (upstream, own operations, downstream) was included in the assessment.

[ESRS 2-IRO-1.53h] For the 2024 Sustainability Statement, OMV Petrom conducted a full Double Materiality Assessment, in accordance with ESRS requirements. The assessment was conducted in 2023 and reviewed in 2024. Compared to the prior reporting periods, this is the first time OMV Petrom has used the ESRS methodology to determine its sustainability materiality. In the previous years, OMV Petrom used the GRI guidelines to determine its sustainability materiality, as disclosed in the specific sections in its previous Sustainability Reports, which are public on the company's website.

OMV Petrom will assess for each reporting period whether material changes have occurred either internally or

externally that could result in changes to its list of material impacts, risks and opportunities, and revise the double materiality process as such. OMV Petrom has not planned the future revision date of the materiality assessment.

### [DR IRO-1 E1] Description of the processes to identify and assess material climate-related impacts, risks and opportunities

[E1-IRO-1.20a, E1-IRO-1.AR 9] The assessment of climaterelated impacts, risks and opportunities, as part of the Double Materiality Assessment, followed the 5-step process described above.

The impact assessment for the climate change topic was performed by the environmental management team. Leveraging expert judgement and historical experience within the company, OMV Petrom's own operations were screened for current and planned activities to identify actual and potential climate change and energy impacts. Related GHG emissions and their sources are described in DR E1-6 \- Gross Scopes 1, 2, 3 and Total GHG emissions section. The process of identification and assessment is following the internal policy framework, as described in DR E1-2 - Policies, which is based on industry specific guidelines and applicable legislation, including but not limited to Intergovernmental Panel on Climate Change (IPCC), IEA, IOGP, IPIECA and Concawe reports.
<!-- Report Page 97 -->

The Strategy department, in collaboration with the environmental management team, assessed the risk and opportunities following the same approach as for impacts.

OMV Petrom's entire value chain was included in the assessment, with the objective to identify actual and potential impacts, as well as risks and opportunities.

[E1-IRO-1.20b i-ii, E1-IRO-1.AR11, E1-IRO-1.AR13, E1- IRO-1.21] OMV Petrom performed an analysis of climaterelated physical risks, focusing on several aspects. This started by selecting a list of climate-related hazards specific to the geographical locations of OMV Petrom's business operations, based on Commission Delegated Regulation (EU) 2021/2139. Next, OMV Petrom's assets and activities were assessed to better understand how these may be impacted by climate change hazards. The approach to risk management was then developed with the awareness that the potential impacts of climate change risks may change over the duration of the business or asset's life.

With the support of an external consultant, OMV Petrom's assets were modeled for climate-related physical risks, focusing on perils including surface water and riverine

flooding, coastal inundation, soil movement, extreme wind, wildfire, freeze-thaw, and extreme heat. The climate change model projects how selected climate-related perils could evolve over time and quantifies the physical damage that could be expected for the asset portfolio. The peril severity is mapped with the percentage of the property damage for each asset included in the analysis, to understand the potential estimated financial loss, considering the standardized archetypes used to represent OMV Petrom asset specifications. The climate-related physical risk assessment assumes no significant changes in the replacement value of the assets at risk.

The frequency and severity of the climate-related hazards were determined according to the following IPCC Representative Concentration Pathways (RCP): RCP 8.5, a high emission scenario referred to as "business as usual" and RCP 4.5, an intermediate emission scenario where global emissions peak around 2040 and decline, stabilizing greenhouse gas concentration by 2100.

For 2024, the result of the climate-related physical risk assessment indicates that the assets analyzed will be largely unaffected by any of the climate-related hazards until the middle of the century. Up to 2030, the OMV Petrom assets analyzed will not need to adapt to unavoidable impacts of climate change, which confirms that the OMV Petrom portfolio is climate-resilient until 2030.

For the supply chain, an indication of exposure to climaterelated physical risks was obtained using a set of climate change risk indicators mapped to the suppliers and their geographical location, in conjunction with the type of services and products supplied.

The outcome of this analysis supports the Double Materiality Assessment.

[E1-IRO-1.20c i-ii, E1-IRO-1.AR12, E1-IRO-1.21] Please see information in Climate change chapter, section, [E1-SBM3.19].

[E1-IRO-1.AR 15] For further information on base case and IEA "Net Zero Emissions by 2050" scenarios, please refer to Note 2 to the Consolidated financial statements for the yearend December 31, 2024.

### [DR IRO-1 E2] Description of the processes to identify and assess material pollution-related impacts, risks and opportunities

[E2-IRO-1.11a] The assessment of impacts, risks and opportunities related to pollution as part of the Double

Materiality Assessment followed the 5-step process described above.

The assessment has been concluded based on industry specifics and considering the internal formal assessment for environmental risks, which are done under the provisions of Environmental Management Standard. During the process, OMV Petrom's site locations and business activities were assessed for identification of actual and potential impacts, risks and opportunities. This screening is grounded in past experiences with incidents, meaning particular attention was given to activities and sites where impacts have occurred before, incidents which are recorded in the company's reporting system. The impacts, risks and opportunities were assessed based on the environmental governance framework, with business division-level information considered for the top-down qualitative assessment.

<!-- Report Page 98 -->
The value chain was also included in the assessment.

[E2-IRO-1.11b] We used the conclusions of our consultations with affected communities, to understand their concerns, needs and priorities. These consultations are conducted through various communication channels, including public forums and/or direct discussions, to ensure transparent and inclusive communication. In addition, affected communities, through their proxies, were also involved during the Double Materiality Assessment process, as described in ESRS 2-IRO-1.53a.

[E2-IRO-1.AR 4] The Double Materiality Assessment was performed at sub-topic level, as described in ESRS 1.AR16.

[E2-IRO-1.AR 9] Our material impacts arise from business activities related to the production and processing of oil and gas. Such locations include the Petrobrazi refinery, the oil and gas production units within Exploration & Production segment, and the Brazi power plant.

### [DR IRO-1 E3] Description of the processes to identify and assess material water and marine resources-related impacts, risks and opportunities

[E3-IRO 1.8] The assessment of impacts, risks and opportunities related to water and marine resources as part of the Double Materiality Assessment followed the 5-step process described above.

[E3-IRO 1.8a] The assessment was performed by OMV Petrom's environmental management team of experts. In the process, our site locations and business activities were assessed for actual and potential impacts, risks and opportunities.

The impacts, risks and opportunities were assessed based on a robust environmental governance framework, with business division-level information considered for the topdown qualitative assessment, including the value chain, with details described in ESRS 2-IRO-1.53a-c.

To identify activities in areas at risk of water scarcity or water stress, international tools and indexes including Verisk Maplecroft's "Water Stress Index" as well as other assessment tools were considered, with the aim to screen the state of nature including water resource:

EUROSTAT WEI+ water exploitation index (included in national Water Management Plan at river basin).

Water Footprint Assessment (WFA) Tool, based on Hogeboom's global model for water quantity.

Worldwide Fund for Nature (WWF) Water Risk Filter, evaluation tool for areas at water risk.

Risk assessments conducted so far indicate a minimum to medium water risk level. The assessment process to scientifically delimit areas at water risk, is ongoing, with further details to be analysed also in other tools such as the WWF Water Risk Filter.

[E3-IRO 1.8b] We used the conclusions of our regular consultations with affected communities, to understand their concerns, needs and priorities. Our engagement process with the affected communities is described in the Affected communities chapter. In addition, affected communities through their proxies were also involved during the Double Materiality Assessment process.
<!-- Report Page 99 -->

[E3-IRO.1.AR 15] The Double Materiality Assessment results will be consolidated with a more specific approach in the next years.

### [DR IRO-1 E4] Description of the processes to identify and assess material biodiversity and ecosystems- related impacts, risks and opportunities

[E4-IRO 1.17a] The assessment of impacts, risks and opportunities related to Biodiversity and ecosystems as part of the Double Materiality Assessment followed the 5-step process described above.

Our own site locations and the value chain were included in the analysis. The assessment has been concluded on industry specifics and considering the internal formal assessment for environmental risks, which are done under the provisions of Environmental Management Standards.

[E4-IRO 1.17b, E4-IRO 1.17d] The Double Materiality Assessment results will be consolidated with a more specific approach in the next years, which will follow the guidance and phases of Locate, Evaluate, Assess, Prepare (LEAP) as recommended by Taskforce on Nature-related Financial Disclosures (TNFD).

[E4-IRO 1.17c] Following the same scenario used for climate change analysis, IPCC highlighted the way climate change could alter the ecosystems, and loss of biodiversity exacerbated by pollution or land use change.

Biodiversity initiatives that OMV Petrom commits to are meant to ensure ecosystems protection or restorations that reduces the risk to biodiversity. Also, the company commits to further understand the impact of fossil fuel-based business on biodiversity. Details are included in DR E4-3, Biodiversity and ecosystems chapter.

[E4-IRO 1.17e i-ii] We used the conclusions of our consultations with affected communities, to understand their concerns, needs and priorities. Communities were involved in the Double Materiality Assessment, as described in [ESRS 2-IRO-1.53a].

[E4-IRO 1.17e iii] The company has not identified any actual or potential impact related to ecosystem services. As such, [E4-IRO 1.17e iii] is not applicable.

[E4-IRO 1.19a] We operate inside various types of National Protected Areas (NPA), Natura 2000 and Key Biodiversity Areas. For a clear determination of whether our activities related to these sites negatively affect these areas, we have started an evaluation and assessment over all activities, following a LEAP assessment guided by TNFD and considering local and national public information on the state of nature for each regional area. The assessment is in progress. For more details regarding the progress on the TNFD LEAP assessment, please see Biodiversity and ecosystems chapter.

[E4-IRO 1.19b] Impacts and mitigation measures are defined in accordance with the permits obtained for each of our sites. Additional measures implemented are included in ISO 14001 Environmental Management System (EMS). Mitigation measures are applied for significant impacts identified during the regulatory assessment procedure. Permitting and assessment procedures are applicable in all countries in which OMV Petrom Group operates.

When significant impacts are observed or predicted, the mitigation hierarchy is followed, and action planning

prioritizes the avoidance and minimization of impacts rather than restoration and offsetting.

[E4-IRO-1.AR 4-6] The Double Materiality Assessment was performed at sub-topic level, as described in ESRS 1.AR16. Details are included in ESRS 2-IRO-1 section.
<!-- Report Page 100 -->

### EU Taxonomy

The EU Taxonomy Regulation, established under Regulation (EU) 2020/852, provides a classification framework for environmentally sustainable economic activities. OMV Petrom's reporting aligns with this regulation, emphasizing its role in identifying eligible and aligned activities that contribute to the EU's environmental objectives. The Complementary Climate Delegated Act (Regulation (EU) 2022/1214), which includes provisions for certain gas and nuclear energy activities, has been integrated into the assessment methodology. This update reflects OMV Petrom's commitment to adhering to the most recent regulatory advancements.

In June 2023, the European Commission adopted the Taxonomy Environmental Delegated Act, establishing technical screening criteria for economic activities contributing to the remaining four environmental objectives (Sustainable use and protection of water and marine resources, Transition to a circular economy, Pollution prevention and control, Protection and restoration of biodiversity and ecosystems). These criteria, published in the Official Journal on November 21, 2023, have been applied starting with January 2024. Additionally, the Taxonomy Climate Delegated Act was amended to include further economic activities in the manufacturing and transport sectors that contribute to climate change mitigation and adaptation. These developments have been incorporated into OMV Petrom's reporting framework for the upcoming period.

### OMV Petrom's process for identifying and assessing EU Taxonomy activities

<!-- Report Page 102 -->
#### EU Taxonomy eligibility assessment

An economic activity is eligible for the EU Taxonomy if it matches the specified activity description provided in the EU Taxonomy Delegated Acts. OMV Petrom undertook a comprehensive screening of its portfolio of activities for comparison against the descriptions of the economic activities outlined in Annex I or II of the EU Taxonomy Climate Delegated Act and in Annexes I-IV of the EU Taxonomy Delegated Acts. This assessment used both bottom-up and top-down approach, involving interdisciplinary teams (both corporate and business segments) for effective identification of eligible activities, assets, projects, and their corresponding capital and operational expenditure and turnover.

All OMV Petrom's identified EU Taxonomy-eligible economic activities are directly related to the environmental objective of climate change mitigation. Analysis of all our economic activities is done on an annual basis and includes an update of the previous year assessment.

#### EU Taxonomy alignment assessment

OMV Petrom's alignment assessment includes the following criteria:

Substantial contribution to climate change mitigation. Evaluations include the application of technical screening criteria for eligible activities to ensure they contribute meaningfully to climate objectives. Evidence is collected by project managers, supported by the Sustainability sub-department, for each eligible activity. Few examples of such activities are electricity generation using solar photovoltaic technology and infrastructure enabling lowcarbon road transport public transport.

"Do No Significant Harm" (DNSH) criteria: Compliance is assessed for each DNSH objective, covering water and marine resources, circular economy, pollution prevention and control, biodiversity, and ecosystems. Physical and climate risk assessments for DNSH are conducted centrally, by sustainability experts, using the EnterpriseWide Risk Management approach, with external validation where necessary.

Minimum social safeguards: Compliance with international standards, including human rights, labor law, anti-corruption policies, and tax governance, is ensured through an evaluation of OMV Petrom's key policies, such as the Human Rights Policy, Code of Conduct, and Tax Strategy. Processes such as grievance mechanisms and community consultations are reviewed to ensure conformity with global standards.

To ensure comprehensive assessments aligned with EU Taxonomy standards, the evaluation process involves corporate functions, business segments representatives (such as sustainability experts, project managers) and external consultants.

##### Definition of financial KPIs

OMV Petrom's Key Performance Indicators (KPIs) are calculated based on OMV Petrom Group's consolidated IFRS financial statements. KPIs are computed based on the consolidated sales revenues (equivalent with the turnover indicator from taxonomy), CAPEX, and OPEX of OMV

Petrom SA and its fully consolidated subsidiaries. Subsidiaries that are not fully consolidated, associated companies and joint ventures were excluded from the assessment. These KPIs (turnover, CAPEX, OPEX) reflect the eligibility and alignment of economic activities under the EU Taxonomy framework and are derived from consolidated IFRS financial statements. The following principles and methodologies based on the Delegated Act 2021/2178 for Reporting („Disclosure Delegated Act") apply:

The proportion of taxonomy-aligned economic activities in the sales revenues, CAPEX, and OPEX (the "alignment ratio") has been calculated as the part of sales revenues, CAPEX, and OPEX derived from products and services associated with taxonomy-aligned economic activities (numerator) divided by the total sales revenues, CAPEX, and OPEX (denominator). The same logic applies to the calculation of the "eligibility ratio".

Turnover Denominator: Based on OMV Petrom's consolidated sales revenues, as reported in the OMV Petrom Consolidated Financial Statements (2024, Note 19). For further details on our accounting policies regarding consolidated sales revenues, see OMV Petrom Consolidated Financial Statements 2024, Note 5.3.q).

CAPEX Denominator: Includes additions to intangible assets, tangible assets (excluding decommissioning costs), and right-of-use assets. It includes also the additions due to business combinations (except for goodwill which is excluded from the CAPEX KPI). Relevant details are

included in Notes 7 and 8 of the Consolidated Financial Statements (2024). Additional details on accounting policies are described in Notes 5.3.d), e), f), and g).

OPEX Denominator: Consists of research and development (R&D) expenses, maintenance and repair costs, other direct expenditures for day-to-day servicing of assets, and shortterm leases.

R&D expenses under "Other operating expenses" in the income statement include the research and development expenses. These expenses are detailed in the Consolidated Financial Statements (2024, Notes 22 and 5.3.h).

Maintenance and repair costs and other direct expenditure related to day-to-day servicing of assets are found under "Production and operating expenses" and "Selling, distribution, and administrative expenses" lines in the income statement.

Expenses for short-term leases are determined and accounted for in accordance with IFRS 16 "Leases".

Direct costs for training and human resources improvements are excluded due to immateriality.

In cases where joint production occurs (e.g., fuels and organic basic chemicals), allocations are made using specific keys that reflect plant yield, size, and operational complexity (Activity 3.14).

##### Taxonomy-eligible and aligned turnover:

In 2024, 8.63% of OMV Petrom's turnover could be classified as taxonomy eligible (2023: 7.51%) and 0.02% as taxonomy aligned turnover (N/A in 2023).

Taxonomy-eligible turnover 2024: Taxonomy-eligible turnover reflects revenues derived from activities that meet the EU Taxonomy's eligibility criteria. These activities include mainly the electricity generation from fossil gaseous fuels in OMV Petrom own facilities (Activity 4.29), manufacture of organic basic chemicals (Activity 3.14) and

high-efficiency co-generation of heat/cool and power from fossil gaseous fuels (Activity 4.30).

Taxonomy-aligned turnover 2024: Aligned turnover includes revenues generated from eligible activities that comply with the technical screening criteria, DNSH criteria, and minimum social safeguards. The methodology follows the Group's guidance, ensuring no double counting and proper allocation keys for joint production activities. In 2024 aligned turnover derives from infrastructure enabling lowcarbon transport (Activity 6.15).

##### Taxonomy-eligible and aligned CAPEX:

In 2024, 14.54% of OMV Petrom's CAPEX qualified as taxonomy-eligible (2023: 19.92%), and 14.49% was deemed taxonomy-aligned (2023: 1.57%).

Taxonomy-eligible CAPEX 2024: Eligible CAPEX includes investments in tangible and intangible assets related to eligible activities, such as manufacture of organic basic chemicals (Activity 3.14), sea and coastal freight water transport, vessels for port operations and auxiliary activities (Activity 6.10) and electricity generation from fossil gaseous fuels (Activity 4.29).These investments are directly linked to the EU Taxonomy activities identified during the eligibility assessment.

Taxonomy-aligned CAPEX 2024: Aligned CAPEX reflects investments in eligible activities that meet alignment criteria,

including DNSH and social safeguards. The calculation excludes decommissioning costs.

In line with our strategy and our plan to diversify our portfolio with greener investments, we have enhanced our focus on the production of biofuels, on renewable energy projects and on upgrading and modernizing the existing infrastructure to improve efficiency and reduce emissions. Therefore, the significant increase in taxonomy-aligned CAPEX compared to previous years, highlights the company's commitment to sustainable investments and adherence to the EU Taxonomy for sustainable activities.

The activities contributing most to aligned CAPEX for climate change mitigation were the activities related to electricity generation using solar photovoltaic technology (Activity 4.1), infrastructure enabling low-carbon road transport (Activity

## 6.15) and public transport and manufacture of hydrogen (Activity 3.1).

#### Climate change

<!-- Report Page 106 -->
At OMV Petrom, the commitment to transitioning to a lower carbon future is aligned with our Strategy 2030, as well as our dedication to achieve long-term growth by capitalizing on higher demand for green energy and on investment opportunities across the value chain while reducing the transition risks.

Our 2030 GHG emissions reduction targets were developed with focus on reducing our impact and the carbon footprint of our operations and portfolio.

Our strategic projects, including Neptun Deep and low and zero carbon business portfolio, support our company's and Romania's decarbonization agenda, as well as our ambition to reach net zero operations by 2050 (Scopes 1 and 2). Furthermore, we have set GHG emissions reduction targets by 2030, enabled by the decarbonization of our current operations through modernization and optimization. Deployment of Carbon Capture and Storage (CCS) is fundamental to achieving the net emissions reduction target.
