## Strategy [DR SBM-3] Material impacts, risks and opportunities and their interaction with strategy and business model

[S1-SBM 3.13a, S1-SBM 3.13a i] OMV Petrom's Strategy 2030 shows how the company plans to transform for a lower carbon future. The actual and potential impacts on the company's own workforce are connected to its business model and plans, as these can only be put in motion safely and sustainably through the efforts of its employees. The People & Culture and HSSE Strategies are enablers in achieving OMV Petrom's Purpose and Strategy. The company constantly strives to advance positive impacts on its own workforce, related to working conditions offered, their physical and mental wellbeing, development opportunities and healthcare services.

OMV Petrom is fully aware that the high-hazard impact industry, where it operates in comes with increased safety risks, therefore fostering a company culture where HSSE shapes decision-making at every level is paramount.

The company aims at shaping an adaptable, innovative, efficient, and resilient organization, based on modern structures that function with lean processes, using efficient interfaces and digital solutions, which are adapted to an industry transitioning to Low carbon and renewable business solutions.

[S1-SBM 3.13a ii] During its double materiality assessment, OMV Petrom identified the need to provide consistent upskilling and career development opportunities in line with the business strategic goals, within a healthy work environment favoring well-being and inclusiveness, and facilitating cooperation and innovation. Creating a culture of continuous learning, with focus on digital and technical proficiency as well on transformational leadership capabilities, is one of the main pillars OMV Petrom relies on to deliver sustainable performance in its transition to low and zero carbon objectives.

[S1-SBM 3.13b] On its transition journey, OMV Petrom remains a reliable employer for its own workforce, leased personnel and value chain. As a major employer in the country, the company offers secure employment, flexible working conditions, and future-oriented skills development, thus attracting and retaining a diverse workforce. At the same time, it fosters opportunities for current employees through upskilling and reskilling, leading to better paid jobs. These positive impacts are present throughout OMV Petrom's operations and align with the exemplary labour practices in Europe.

Recognizing the industry's demand for specialized knowledge and skills, OMV Petrom addresses workforce aging, geographic distribution and challenging working conditions in its strategy to attract and retain young talent,

whether onsite or remotely. The company cultivates a pipeline of young experts through active collaboration with vocational schools and universities (please refer to S1-SBM 3.14c for details).

Investing in employee training represents an opportunity for enhanced efficiency, safety and operational performance, leading to streamlined processes, reduced waste, and optimized resource utilization, contributing to a sustainable and resilient business model. Continuous learning fosters innovation, enabling employees to introduce creative solutions that positively impact the business and society, address market demands, solve environmental and social challenges.

A highly skilled workforce enhances customer experience by delivering exceptional service and effectively meeting customer needs. This expertise fosters long-lasting client relationships, strengthens brand loyalty, and elevates the company's reputation.
<!-- Report Page 153 -->

The nature of OMV Petrom's industry exposes employees to chemicals, pollutants, constant noise, light, and other stressors, as well as the risk of injuries ranging from minor to severe, with potential long-term effects or fatalities. Such impacts arise from the company's operational profile and can lead to decreased employee commitment, lost workdays, and reduced productivity. As such, the company's strategy and business model embed clear health and safety considerations, based on rigorous safety protocols, continuous training, implementation of advanced safety technologies and provision of comprehensive healthcare services.

[S1-SBM 3.14] All people in our own workforce who could be materially impacted by OMV Petrom are included in the scope of disclosure under this chapter.

[S1-SBM 3.14a] To carry out its business activities, OMV Petrom directly employs specialized staff: own employees,and, when necessary, based on specific business needs, utilizes leased personnel, classified as nonemployees under ESRS S1 Own workforce.

[S1-SBM 3.14b] In line with the double materiality assessment, with regards to own workforce, the company identified one potential material negative impact that is related to Health & Safety: exposure to chemicals, pollutants, constant noise, light, other stressors as well as low to severe injuries, with short to long term effect or even fatalities, due to operational profile. This impact is related to individual incidents that impact a specific location / operation / period.

[S1-SBM 3.14c] OMV Petrom's labor rights and practices stem from the collective bargaining agreement, with freedom of association serving as a stability pillar and key driver of the positive employee impact. The 2024-2025 collective labour agreement ensures steady and secure employment, increased income predictability, and transparent regulation through social dialogue. The following aspects are set in agreement with the representative trade union: the company minimum wage, the general salary increases, and additional benefits related to commuting, relocation and increments related to working conditions to maintain the appeal of certain positions.

Leased employees benefit from similar rights and working conditions as regular employees, as per the legal requirements.

The high trade union affiliation contributes to the enterprise stability and sustainability, providing a heathy and predictable work environment attentive to employees' needs and aspirations. This also promotes balanced labour relations, social harmony, and a performance-oriented culture.

OMV Petrom is committed to cultivating a highly skilled and knowledgeable workforce through comprehensive training and development programs, supported by an annual review process. The company's learning and development strategy revolves around the identification and development of skills that are essential for business-critical directions and areas, as well as advancing leadership skills.

Education also serves as a crucial instrument for ensuring workers and managers are aware of workplace risks and control measures, enabling them to operate more safely. This educational aspect is detailed in the Group Standard "HSSE Awareness and Competencies", which specifies the HSSE prerequisites for different classifications of employees. The new "Transformational Leadership Competencies" were developed to complete the business transformational framework. They define the behaviours expected from the company's leaders across the Group.

In the area of health, OMV Petrom has designed and implemented health services, programs, and campaigns targeting major health conditions, while raising awareness, promoting health, and preventing health complications, in order to advance its positive material impact.

Such examples include the Private Health Insurance program which has provided significant financial support to both employees and non-employees in difficult medical situations, as well as the Health: ON! Platform that has been

serving as a hub for all company-provided health programs and initiatives, both active since 2014.

[S1-SBM 3.14d] The company's traditional people culture relies on opportunities for long-term employment, job security, professional mobility and continuous development, with the employer ensuring favourable working conditions through adequate wages and dedicated benefits. These elements enhance employees' engagement and motivation, boosting operational efficiency and fostering innovation.

The opportunity to provide adequate wages arises from the dependency on a motivated workforce. By committing to locally applicable decent living wages standards, the company ensures that minimum salaries or wages meet legal requirements. Through rewards initiatives, the company plans to consider local cost of living indices, housing costs, healthcare expenses, educational needs and other assumptions in the determination of the salaries. Ensuring adequate wages is OMV Petrom's goal for promoting fair working practices and enhancing employee financial well-being.

The training and development opportunity stems from the company's dependency on a skilled and knowledgeable workforce. By investing in the professional and personal growth of its employees, OMV Petrom cultivates a more efficient and effective workforce. This leads to safe and enhanced operational performance, together with the generation of innovative ideas, and the creation of products and services that resonate with customers, all of which are dependent on the capabilities and growth of the employees.

Please refer to paragraph S1-SBM 3.13b within this chapter for more information regarding the cultural context and dependencies and interventions that transform risks into opportunities.

The risk of injuries (both high and low consequence) is linked to the dependency that the company has on its workforce, namely, on the employees' commitment and productivity. For this reason, OMV Petrom implements health and safety measures to safeguard its employees and maintain the continuity and effectiveness of its operations.

[S1-SBM 3.14e] The energy transition comes with a gradual change in the profile of OMV Petrom's business, necessitating new skills for emerging lines of business, while leveraging existing expertise for areas such as CCS or hydrogen.
<!-- Report Page 154 -->

Recognizing the significance of job security, the company engages in thorough contingency planning. When workplace

adjustments or employment termination are required due to business, organizational, or security changes, OMV Petrom evaluates all the options and engages in constructive dialogue with maximum consideration.

The company focuses on digital solutions and automations across all business areas, creating new job opportunities and attracting new talent, while maintaining new development opportunities for existing employees (via upskilling and reskilling). The digitalization and automation allow remote supervision of technologies, making jobs accessible to a diverse workforce.OMV Petrom invests in education (through partnerships with vocational schools and universities), supporting the creation of a reliable and up-todate pipeline of people and specialists that aligns with the company's strategy.

[S1-SBM 3.14f] OMV Petrom has no operations at significant risk of incidents of forced labour or compulsory labour. Forced labour or compulsory labour is banned and punishable by law in Romania, the country where our significant operations are focused.

[S1-SBM 3.14g] OMV Petrom has no operations at significant risk of incidents of child labour. The compay's major operations are in Romania, which has ratified the most relevant treaties regarding labour rights (e.g. Abolition of Forced Labour Convention No. 105, Minimum Age Convention No. 138) and its domestic legislation is robust (e.g. the minimum age for employment is not less than 15, hazardous work is prohibited for people aged under 18 and the law provides a comprehensive prohibition of the worst forms of child labour).

Additionally, the Human Rights Matrix addresses forced labour and child labour, which are also topics covered through human rights trainings for all employees.

[S1-SBM 3.15] OMV Petrom considers the following categories as vulnerable / at risk people in its own workforce: women and young employees. Traditionally, the oil and gas industry has been male dominated, particularly in blue-collar roles. OMV Petrom also operates in geographic areas where working conditions are less attractive to young people.

In order to gain an understanding of the needs of vulnerable groups within its own workforce, the company ensures open communication with its employees and their representatives under social dialogue, gathers data and considers incidents reported through the grievance mechanism and social dialogue, while trying to find mutual beneficial solutions via collective negotiations and good implementation of the

existing processes. These processes support professional flexibility and job security for all employees, including the most vulnerable.

In its efforts to understand and mitigate risks of harm to employees with specific characteristics, OMV Petrom fosters an inclusive culture through the implementation of antidiscrimination regulations, a grievance mechanism, and initiatives that celebrate diversity. This allows the creation of a larger pipeline for diverse workforce, alleviating eventual generational gaps. The company concentrates on technologization, digitalization and automation, to transform traditionally male-dominated jobs for women and young experts.

[S1-SBM 3.16] OMV Petrom's material risks and opportunities pertaining to own workforce are related to all its employees, and do not result from impacts and dependencies on specific groups of people.

## Impact, risk and opportunity management [DR S1-1] Policies related to own workforce

[S1-1.19] Our impacts, risks and opportunities are considered in our internal regulations, with the goal to set clear and transparent rules, as part of our management system. These regulations include:

### Code of Conduct

[MDR-P-a] The OMV Petrom Group Code of Conduct serves as a compulsory conduct guideline applicable to all our employees. It lays down OMV Petrom's specific commitments on transition to low carbon business, run responsible operations, foster people and communities, ethical business practices, corporate values and existing reporting channels.

[MDR-P (b-f)] For more details, please refer to the Climate change chapter.

## Human Rights Policy Statement

<!-- Report Page 155 -->
The Human Rights Policy Statement sets out OMV Petrom's commitment to adhere to the highest standards in order to provide its employees safe workplaces, as well as the company's commitment to ensure a high level of care for employees' well-being, physical and mental health across the Group [MDR-P-a].

[MDR-P (b-f)] For more details, please refer to the Climate change chapter.

## HSSE Directive

[MDR-P-a] The OMV Petrom HSSE Directive establishes the principles and rules for managing Health, Safety, Security & Resilience, and Environment (HSSE) across all stages of the company's business and activities, including capital projects and mergers and acquisitions. It serves as a framework for managing HSSE aspects with the aim of safeguarding people, the environment, assets, and the company's reputation, in accordance with OMV Petrom's HSSE Policy and its vision and mission of "Committed to Zero Harm - Protect People, Environment and Assets".

[MDR-P (b-f)] For more details, please refer to the the Climate change chapter.

## HSSE Policy

[MDR-P-a, b, c, f] The HSSE Policy states that the long-term business success of OMV Petrom depends on its ability to continually improve and ensure the quality of its activities, products, processes, and services while protecting people, environment, assets and reputation; therefore, health, safety, security, environment (HSSE) are integral parts of the business. The policy covers OMV Petrom Group. The Executive Board of OMV Petrom is the most senior level accountable for the implementation of the HSSE Policy. The requirements of the HSSE Policy are communicated through internal platforms, including training when major revisions of the HSSE Policy are undertaken.

## Occupational Safety Management Standard

This Standard defines the minimum requirements for effective occupational safety management in OMV Petrom. Occupational safety management is an integral part of the overall approach to manage HSSE and includes the systematic use of uniform instructions, practices and specifications aimed at achieving and maintaining safe work. The fundamental components include the organization, resources, management processes, people and equipment performance, the prevailing safety culture along with documented regulations and practices. The aim of Occupational Safety Management is to enable the organization to improve occupational safety practices, behaviour and performance [MDR-P-a]. The Standard covers OMV Petrom Group[MDR-P-b]. Each line manager in their area of responsibility is accountable for the implementation of the standard [MDR-P-c]. The requirements of the Occupational Safety Management Standard are communicated through internal platforms, including training when major revisions of the standard are undertaken [MDR-P-f].

## Health Management Regulation

This regulation defines the minimum requirements for health protection and promotion within the OMV Petrom Group. The purpose of this Group Standard is to describe the main principles, the roles and responsibilities as well as the communication protocols within OMV Petrom Group, providing a framework for managing all health-related aspects and ensuring collaboration with other HSSE specialists [MDR-P-a]. The scope of the policy, accountability for the implementation of the policy and methods for making the policy available to stakeholders are the same as for the Occupational Safety Management Standard [MDR-P-b, MDR-P-c, MDR-P-f].

## HR Directive

The Directive outlines the organizational structure and overview on People & Culture ("P&C") functions responsible for the diligent implementation of all people processes, in line with OMV Petrom's strategy. Its annexes cover aspects such as business ethics, non-discrimination and equal opportunities, anti-harassment standards, as well as the transparent process for handling labour-related grievances. The regulation helps mitigate reputational risks and ensures a transparent communication channel for complaints [MDRP-a]. The Directive applies at Group level, ensuring aggregated and unified responsibilities throughout OMV Petrom Group, while also addressing specific local competencies [MDR-P-b]. The P&C Vice president is accountable for the implementation of the Directive [MDR-Pc]. The regulation is aligned with European and local legislation and best practices [MDR-P-d], and is available on the company Intranet and the communication channel as part of the Integrity platform [MDR-P-f].

## CLA and related processes, Internal Regulation and Related Guidelines

These regulations encompass most wage-related articles, including minimum wage and total remuneration. The CLA includes provisions for long-term employment, internal job opportunities (vacancies), flexible work-ing solutions (workplace, working time, rest time), work-life balance benefits, and aspects related to secure employment (income compensations during medical leaves, in case of job loss, in case of work incapacity). These regulations form OMV Petrom's Employer of Choice offer by providing competitive salaries and good working conditions, and opportunities [MDR-P-a]. The regulations primarily apply to all employees meeting the conditions described therein, with some aspects tailored to certain working conditions, jobs or business areas, mostly for retention purposes [MDR-P-b]. EB

Members are accountable for the implementation of these regulations [MDR-P-c], which adhere to market best practices [MDR-P-d]. The availability is ensured through the company Intranet [MDR-P-f].

## Personnel Relocation Standard

The Standard addresses job opportunities driven by the enterprise or employees, supported by relocation and commuting benefits for employees and their family members [MDR-P-a]. This Standard supports all employees meeting certain conditions [MDR-P-b]. All senior managers are mainly accountable for implementing this Standard [MDR-Pc], which aligns with market best practices [MDR-P-d]. The method for making the Standard available to stakeholders is the same as for the CLA & related processes, Internal Regulation and Related Guidelines [MDR-P-f].

<!-- Report Page 156 -->
## Teleworking Procedure

The Procedures address the implementation of new ways of working, including the possibility of working remotely from home locations, and aims to increase labour relations flexibility and enhance work-life bal-ance [MDR-P-a] for all employees [MDR-P-b]. Responsibility for the implementation of this procedure lies with the EB member [MDR-P-c]. The procedure adheres to market best practices and European standards [MDR-P-d]. The method for making the Procedure available to stakeholders is the same as for the CLA & related processes, Internal Regulation and Related Guidelines [MDR-P-f].

## Commuting Procedure

The Procedure addresses the simplification of commuting conditions and reimbursement. It supports commuting to and from workplaces to home locations [MDR-P-a] for all employees [MDR-P-b]. Responsibility for the implementation of this procedure lies with the managers of each department where the employees are part off [MDR-P-c]. The method for making the Procedure available to stakeholders is the same as for the CLA & related processes, Internal Regulation and Related Guidelines [MDR-P-f].

## Grievance Mechanism (supported by Internal Regulation and P&C Directive)

The OMV Petrom grievance mechanism is implemented through several accessible channels, including the Whistleblowing Platform. The set-up is meant to ensure effective communication to management regarding employee needs and claims [MDR-P-a]. The mechanism applies across all OMV Petrom Group [MDR-P-b], and the responsibility for its implementation lies with the Vice President of P&C [MDR-P-c]. Through this mechanism, OMV Petrom commits to respect the European and national regulations on whistleblowing and grievances [MDR-P-d]. The availability of the mechanism is ensured via the company's Intranet [MDR-P-f].

[S1-1.20, S1-1.20a] The Human Rights Policy Statement outlines the company's commitment to maintaining human rights in its business operations. This commitment is a guiding principle for handling human rights issues in its daily business matters, comprising specific obligations like workers' rights, protective measures, vulnerable groups, environmental and climate change effects, as well as the Due Diligence Process in place, grievance management, awareness, and communication.

OMV Petrom Group is committed to respecting the human rights and labor rights of all employees, as out-lined in the Universal Declaration of Human Rights (UDHR), the International Labour Organization (ILO) conventions, and the United Nations Guiding Principles on Business and Human Rights (UNGPs).

[S1-1.20b] The company constantly informs and consults with employee representatives regarding developments in its activities that may affect their interests. All details of collective bargaining agreements are communicated through OMV Petrom's internal platform. The People and Culture (P&C) local teams actively keep leaders and employees informed. The P&C operating model and integrated project approach involves concept proposal by relevant function, followed by feedback integration from various stakeholders, ensuring both co-creation of tools and high adaption to organizational needs, thus resulting in more efficient people processes. MySF and Employee Self-Service are internal applications used for P&C and personnel administration, available for direct communication with employees. A formal grievance channel is also available under Integ-rity platform for serious claims such as, but not limited to discrimination, equal chances and harassment.

[S1-1.20c] OMV Petrom's grievance mechanisms is a key tool for preventing and managing negative impacts on local

communities, employees, and other stakeholders.Each grievance is thoroughly investigated and addressed in a non-judicial manner, whether they are based on real or perceived issues, and whether the complainant is known or unknown, following the UN Effectiveness Criteria. Following the UN Guiding Principles on Business and Human Rights, the Human Rights Due Diligence Process involves four key steps:

Evaluating both current and potential human rights impacts linked to the company's present and future business operations

Integrating and acting upon the findings in the company's activities

Monitoring the effectiveness of the company's actions

Communicating on how these impacts are being addressed

The company is committed to a rightsholders' perspective, ensuring that any genuine or potential impact on our stakeholders' rights is professionally examined and acted upon accordingly. This ongoing due diligence process thus makes use of external resources and expertise, and involves external stakeholders, in particular impacted groups.

[S1-1.21] OMV Petrom respects human rights as outlined in the Universal Declaration of Human Rights and globally recognized treaties, including the International Labor Organization (ILO) core treaties. Having signed the UN Global Compact, we are fully committed to the UN Guiding Principles on Business and Human Rights and the OECD Guidelines for Multinational Enterprises.

[S1-1.22, S1-1.23] The HSSE Directive provides a framework for how OMV Petrom manages all HSSE aspects in order to protect people, environment, assets and reputation in line with its HSSE Policy and Major Accident Prevention Policy and in view of its HSSE Vision and Mission "Committed to Zero Harm Protect People, Environment and Assets".
<!-- Report Page 157 -->

[S1-1.24a] Principles on non-discrimination, equal opportunities, and prevention of harassment are subject to multiple regulations that establish interdictions, specific obligations and corrective measures and non-retaliation. Such regulations include the P&C Directive which is managed at local level through the CLA & Internal Regulation. Any misconduct related to the above aspects can be reported by employees claims via the Integrity Platform and are treated under established grievance mechanisms. When necessary, such cases may lead to disciplinary measures in accordance with existing processes.

[S1-1.24b] As per its Code of Conduct, OMV Petrom understands equality and non-discrimination as the cornerstone of the human rights edifice and supports the idea that no one shall be treated differently, in particular less favorable than others, simply because of characteristics such as gender, age, nationality, language, religion, political opinion, sexual orientation or ethnic origin. Additionally, in its Human Rights Policy Statement, OMV Petrom is also committed to equality, regardless of any ground such as race, gender, sexual identity, age, color, ethnic or social origin, genetic features, language, religion or belief, political or any other opinion, membership of a national minority, indigenous origin, property, family status, disability, health status, including mental health, or any other status.

[S1-1.24c] OMV Petrom provides positive action for people from groups at particular risk of vulnerability (protection of females and young people, protection of pregnant women in the context of working conditions) in line with the CLA provisions. The company has adopted measures to advance positive action, such as:

Advancing equal opportunity: being mindful of gender equality in recruitment, hiring, and promo-tion practices.

Supportive programs for career development and advancement for women, work-life balance, ma-ternity and parenting.

Protective working conditions/interdictions to expose to difficult working conditions for young em-ployees and women.

[S1-1.24d] Principles on non-discrimination, equal chances, against any form of harassment, non-retaliation in case of whistleblowing, are subject to multiple regulations, by setting interdictions, specific obligations and correc-tion mechanism. OMV Petrom ensures the implementation of these principles through:

Grievance Mechanism

Disciplinary Misconduct process and regulation

Training and Awareness

Monitoring and Evaluation

## [DR S1-2] Processes for engaging with own workforce and workers' representatives about impacts

[S1-2.27, S1-2.27a] OMV Petrom informs, consults and aligns with employee representatives regarding developments in the company's activities that may affect their interests, on policies, regulations and procedures that may impact employee relations. Such aspects include working time/rest time general models, rules and principles for granting important benefits, important changes of

activities and jobs and their implications on people, reorganization and restructuring related mitigation and attenuation measures.

The Pulse Check is one of the company's most important tools to receive direct feedback from employees on aspects related to company culture, safety and others. Direct engagement occurs also at employee events with Executive Board members and other senior management, where information about the progress made on OMV Petrom's Strategy 2030 is shared. Direct engagement also occurs through the HSSE Walks that all managers are encouraged to perform, in order to have an open and structured dialogue about working conditions and safety culture. In order to encourage and enable the consultation and cooperation between employer and employees on safety and health matters, the company organizes quarterly Health and Safety Committees at different organizational levels.

[S1-2.27b] Constant information, consultation and alignment is performed between employer's and employees' representatives with the view to include employees' perspective into business decisions depending on the type of topic (CLA negotiations, interpretation, and implementation of the CLA, strategic measures concerning employees, projects and initiatives, daily business).

People and Culture (P&C) local teams actively keep leaders and employees informed. The P&C operating model and integrated project approach involves concept proposal by relevant function, followed by feedback integration from various stakeholders, ensuring both co-creation of tools and high adaption to organizational needs, thus resulting in more efficient people processes.

[S1-2.27c] Social dialogue within the company is conducted at multiple organizational levels, including the Supervisory Board (SB), Executive Board (EB), individual EB members, or specific business areas. This dialogue is facilitated by a dedicated organizational structure within the People & Culture function and occurs on a regular basis or in response to specific events.
<!-- Report Page 158 -->

In terms of health and safety, it is the duty of line management at every level of the organization to engage in transparent communication with employees about safety performance, insights gained from past experiences, potential risks and opportunities for enhancement, within their respective areas of responsibility.

[S1-2.27d] The implementation of a new collective labour agreement for 2024 - 2025 has commenced, providing a

predictable frame of labour relations with mutual benefits for employees and employer.

The outcome of the collective negotiation integrated employee's new interest in market trends, such as: ethical labour relations, flexible working conditions, simplification, work-life balance, low carbon. This agreement allows the company to gain valuable insights into the perspectives of own workforce, ensuring that their human rights are respected, and their evolving needs are met.

[S1-2.27e] At the end of each collective negotiation process, effective social dialogue within OMV Petrom and companies within Petrom Group is facilitated through the closing of the CLA and smooth implementation of the deriving processes, some of them requiring trade unions involvement, in all forms (information, consultation, agreement), depending on the topic. The social dialogue is deployed regularly through events at all levels of the company, covering all business areas.

Written communication with trade unions is monitored and addressed, aiming to support a fair and trans-parent social dialogue and to monitor the effectiveness of engagement within the company.

Pulse Check is part of OMV Petrom's Speak-up culture which encourages employees to express their feed-back within a safe and anonymous environment, via a dedicated platform.

In 2024, a rate of 87% in participation was achieved, with around 8,000 employees choosing to have their say. The Engagement score reflecting employee's perception on Engagement ("Say" about company, "Stay" within the company and "Strive"- perform with the company) was 81%, reflecting high motivation and commitment for achieving our business purpose.

With regards to safety, OMV Petrom supports an open reporting and learning culture, where everyone has the confidence to intervene, to stop unsafe work, to speak up, and feedback always is welcomed. Positive contributions to HSSE are recognized and rewarded and fair and transparent consequences for rule violations are applied. Additionally, walkarounds are performed to encourage direct engagement, and quarterly Health & Safety Commitees are organised for employees consultation.

[S1-2.28] Steps have been taken to gain insights of generations, women and disabled employees', by:

Identification of vulnerable groups (see also S1-SBM 3.15)

Tailored engagement initiatives

Collaboration with workers' representatives

Training and awareness programs

Feedback mechanisms

## [DR S1-3] Processes to remediate negative impacts and channels for own workforce to raise concerns

[S1-3.32, S1-3.32a] The aim of OMV Petrom is to assess all complaints received and to handle them with the involvement of dedicated roles and experts. Health & Safety is a subcategory of the social category defined under the grievance mechanism.

<!-- Report Page 159 -->
All complaints are treated in a serious manner, whether they are based on real or perceived issues, irrespective of the identity of the complainant, following the UN Effectiveness Criteria. For more details regarding OMV Petrom's Human Rights Due Diligence Process, please refer S1-1.20c. For information regarding as-sessment and investigation, resolution and remediation, as well as follow-up and feedback, please refer to the Affected communities chapter.

[S1-3.32b, c] There are multiple categories of grievances, including human rights issues, health and safety claims, and complaints, which are addressed through channels such as the Work Health and Safety Committee (CSSM), Synergi, and PetrOmbudsman.

The Grievance Management Process at OMV Petrom Group is integrated in the Compliance Management System, which ensures compliance with applicable legal provisions and internal regulations. Additionally, the company has established Whistleblowing channels, including the Integrity Platform (that fosters a dedicated partition for labour related complaints), Ethics helpline, dedicated email address, direct contact with the Compliance department. The

PetrOmbudsman Department is another channel for own workforce to raise their concerns and receive independent and impartial counselling, in an informal and confidential setup, with services being offered for staff in OMV Petrom Romania, Petrom Moldova, OMV Bulgaria and OMV Ser-bia subsidiaries. The mission of the PetrOmbudsman Department is to help people improve their soft skills, to develop healthy and sustainable working relationships.

[S1-3.33d] The available whistleblowing channels were reinforced quarterly through flyers attached to pay slips, in

or-der to reach frontline employees. Other communication channels with employees are also available via email and Employees' Front Desk, all of them being continuously communicated on the Intranet, via Newsletters and Blogs, while addressing various topics to employees.

[S1-3.32e] OMV Petrom's approach to track and monitor issues raised includes the following steps:

Registering the number of Grievances, by category.

Measuring the number of received grievances versus the number of resolved grievances.

Measuring the number of grievances resolved through remediation.

Every grievance is investigated and resolved in a nonjudicial manner, while providing employees with access to remedies, depending on the case. The Compliance department audits the contents of the database to ensure data accuracy and, where applicable, makes recommendations for improvement. Grievances are closely monitored to guarantee timely resolution.

The Whistleblowing Directive and P&C Directive also ensure that reports or incidents are investigated by personnel who are separated from the chain of management involved in the matter.

[S1-3.33] A Group-wide "Speak-up" channel was launched to assess the level of speak-up culture within the Group.

Where necessary, a deep dive into employee's opinions may be performed via local/structure related surveys to help gain a deeper understanding of specific needs and solutions.

OMV Petrom runs constant awareness campaigns on the importance of whistleblowing:

A dedicated topic on whistleblowing was emphasized during the business ethics trainings.

Info-screen messages were posted to encourage people to report observed misconduct.

OMV Petrom's internal Whistleblowing Directive & P&C Directive stipulate the "no retaliation" policy aiming at protecting individuals who report misconducts in good faith and outline methods for employees and external workers to make confidential and anonymous whistleblower complaints.

## [DR S1-4] Actions

[S1-4-37] OMV Petrom takes actions to address material negative and positive impacts, and to manage material risks

and pursue material opportunities related to its own workforce. The list of actions taken or planned by the company in this regard is presented below:

### Safety

[S1-4-38a] OMV Petrom has adopted actions to prevent and mitigate the material negative impact on employees' health and safety and the risk of injuries.

Education and training for hazard identification and risk awareness & mitigation (cluster including: development of Safety Training Centers, Train the Trainers session for practical Life Saving Rules; e-learning/g-learning for Life Saving Rules, Computer Based training (CBT) modules for HSSE awareness and competencies)

The main expected outcome is to increase the level of knowledge and awareness with regards to hazard identification and risk mitigation [MDR-A-a]. The action is continuous [MDR-A-c] and covers OMV Petrom's own workforce [MDR-A-b].

[S1-4-38d] In terms of progress, in 2024, OMV Petrom organized 6 Safety Training Centers for practical training of Life Saving Rules. 85 trainers have been trained for the delivery of the Life Saving Rules practical training, and 5,406 employees performed the Life Saving Rules e-learning and g-learning. A percentage of ~87% employees vs plan performed CBT [MDR-A-e].

Organization-wide communication of safety alerts and lessons learned.

[MDR-A-a, b, e, 4-38d]. The main expected outcome is to increase the level of awareness of the company's employees in terms of incident prevention. OMV Petrom conducts communications on a case by case basis, and based on planning; these communications cover the company's own workforce. In 2024, 52 investigations were performed for incidents with severity level 3+ (meaning incidents impacting company own or contractors' employees health, environment, assets or reputation) and HiPo (High Potential incidents of levels 4 and 5 that could have led to fatality). Lessons Learned have been developed based on investigations and distributed in accordance with the provisions of the Standard on Reporting, Investigation and Classification of Incidents, resulting in over 1,200 organizational cross-learning activities recorded to Synergi.

Audits & inspections to verify compliance with legal and internal regulations

[MDR-A-a, b, c] The main expected outcome is to identify non-conformities and engage employees in behaviour and work-place safety improvements. Audits and inspections are undertaken as per the audit program approved for each division, and they cover OMV Petrom's own workforce.

[S1-4-38d, MDR-A-e] In terms of progress, in 2024, a total of 13 HSSE Management System Audits were performed, including internal audits for ISO certified entities. Additionally, the company has undertaken 21,310 inspections on compliance with permits to work, 8,898 HSSE Walks and 731 life-saving rules assessments.

### Health & Safety Risk Assessments

The main expected outcome is to identify hazards, assess the risks and establish barriers [MDR-A-a]. These risks assessments are in line with the legal and internal requirements for working conditions [MDR-A-c], they are updated when the working conditions changed, and they cover OMV Petrom's own workforce [MDR-A-b].

[S1-4-38d, MDR-A-e] Risk registers are updated following changes in working conditions.

### Road Safety - In Vehicle Monitoring System and Defensive Driving Training
<!-- Report Page 161 -->

In 2024, we continued to monitor our employees' driving performance and increase the Road Safety awareness via IVMS (In Vehicle Monitoring System), which was again enhanced in 2024 when speed geofences were updated.

[MDR-A-a] Through Defensive Driving training we continue to offer periodical refreshment training and coaching for drivers and enhance their awareness regarding traffic risks exposure. The action covers OMV Petrom's own workforce [MDR-A-b] and is undertaken continuously [MDR-A-c].

[S1-4-38d, MDR-A-e] The results of the monthly data analysis give us the opportunity to further identify necessary improvements and to reward the good behaviors of the drivers.

## Health

[S1-4-38a] OMV Petrom has adopted actions to advance the company's positive impact on employees' health.

Mandatory occupational check-ups

The main expected outcome is to evaluate the health status of OMV Petrom Group own employees [MDR-A-a]. This action is continuous [MDR-A-c], and it covers OMV Petrom's own workforce [MDR-A-b].

[S1-4-38d, MDR-A-e] In terms of progress, in 2024, 100% mandatory occupational medical check-ups were performed, based on the available employee population.

Health ON! Platform - for physical well-being: preventive health programs, flu vaccination, physiotherapy services

[MDR-A-a, b, c] The main expected outcome of the actions taken through the Health ON! Platform is to raise awareness, promote health, and prevent health complications. The actions are continuous, and they cover OMV Petrom's own workforce.

[S1-4-38d, MDR-A-e] The progress is tracked through the voluntary participation of employees to the dedicated health programs.

Health ON! Platform - for mental wellbeing component and psychological support

[MDR-A-a, b, c] Other actions are taken through the Health ON! Platform to advance the company's positive impact on employees' mental wellbeing and offer psychological support, which are the main expected outcomes. The actions are continuous, and they cover OMV Petrom's own workforce in Romania.

### Private health insurance

The main expected outcome is to address medical necessity based on symptomatology of acute and/or chronic illnesses [MDR-A-a]. The action is continuous [MDR-A-c], and it covers OMV Petrom's own workforce [MDR-A-b].

[S1-4-38d] The progress is tracked monthly and quarterly through statistical data provided by the insurer regarding number of employees who accessed the private health insurer. Monitoring the claims data periodically ensures transparency and adherence to insurance industry standards, leads to quicker resolutions and better customer satisfaction and helps in identifying patterns and trends in claims, allowing an up-to-date health statistical information regarding our own workforce [MDR-A-e].

<!-- Report Page 162 -->
## People and culture

[S1-4-38a] OMV Petrom has adopted actions to advance the company's positive impacts and opportunities on employees' working conditions and equal treatment and opportunities.

People days process (PXP review and succession plans)

This is a performance & potential assessment focusing on employees holding positions at a certain levels who's main expected outcomes are to identify and grow OMV Petrom's talent, to promote diversity and mobility, build the leadership pipeline and drive sustainable performance across the Group [MDR-A-a]. This action is undertaken on an annual basis [MDR-A-c], and it covers OMV Petrom's own workforce [MDR-A-b].

[S1-4-38d] In 2024, this assessment process was redesigned to integrate relevant criteria referring to leadership competencies and company values and the timeframe of the whole process cycle was changed to include a second step with focus on development and succession. Therefore, the first step of the process starts in Q4 and ends in March 2025, impacting approximately 1000 employees.

Fresh Graduates Program in E&P business division

The program consists of a comprehensive two-year professional growth plan that includes mentorship, a structured career plan, involvement in pioneering projects, and varied training methods that advance both technical and soft skills through hands-on professional experience, teamwork, and bespoke training sessions. The main expected outcome is to provide a strong foundation for graduates development to launch their career successful, equipping them with skills, experience and confidence. [MDR-A-a].

[S1-4-38d] In 2024, a number of 36 fresh graduates benefited from this program [MDR-A-e].

Talent attraction programs: Open4U, Her energy Academy, OMV Petrom Excellence Scholarship

The Open4U internship program connects young individuals with OMV Petrom, offering a platform to start their careers with expert guidance. Interns explore the energy industry, gain business exposure, and work with experienced teams, acquiring practical knowledge and hands-on experience. Her Energy Academy is a six-month program where students engage in workshops, seminars, and field trips to Petrobrazi Refinery and Petrom City. The focus is on self-awareness,

employability, personal branding, and critical thinking, equipping participants with essential skills for their future careers. The OMV Petrom Excellence Scholarship supports technical students from their second year of study and provides financial assistance and profes-sional development opportunities, fostering academic and professional excellence in technical fields [MDR-A-a]. The programs are organized annually [MDR-A-c], and they target OMV Petrom Group's own workforce [MDR-A-b].

[S1-4-38d] In terms of progress, in 2024, 82 students enjoyed the experience during the 12th edition of Open4Uor. Through HerEnergy Academy, 20 young women were engaged through technical training to facilitate their path to a successful career in the energy sector. 76 high-achieving students benefited from a scholarship through the OMV Petrom Excellence Scholarship, mainly from partnership universities [MDR-A-e].

### AZPC Program (A to Z on P&C)

[S1-4-38d, MDR-A-a, b, e] The A to Z on P&C program aims to improve transparency and understanding of core people processes while fostering closer connections with the organization. To make the recruitment process transparent, easy to understand and to streamline it, the company developed a comprehensive and interactive recruitment elearning in 2024. The program covers OMV Petrom's own workforce. This initiative has been completed by over 160 hiring managers and colleagues.

[MDR-A-e] The "Total Rewards Reveal" project aims to ensure all employees understand the full scope of the current reward framework [MDR-A-a]. The project team trained more than 400 managers in effective communication and decision-making skills through hands-on sessions focused on the newly implemented MySF Compensation module. This initiative has resulted in enhanced transparency & accessibility, with positive feedback encouraging continued efforts and experience sharing among managers.

[MDR-A-e] Local events are organized to promote face-toface interaction with local teams, facilitating direct questions and addressing specific topics. The P&C team travelled to E&P Assets, Petrobrazi Refinery, and Petrom City, engaging in open dialogue with over 550 colleagues. These events, which included panel discussions, Q&A sessions, and interactive workstations on main people processes, involved the entire P&C team from both headquarters and local offices.

[MDR-A-c] This program started in January 2024 with the initiatives above and will continue in 2025.

### Pay gap project

[MDR-A-a, b, c] The main expected outcome is to identify and evaluate pay gap requirements in alignment with CSRD/ESRS and to meet the legal requirements of the EU Directive as well as respective local legislation for OMV Petrom and its subsidiaries from EU countries. It also aims to close the pay gap in accordance with legal require-ments. Existing P&C processes and policies will be revised to reflect the legal requirements. This action is planned annually, and it covers OMV Petrom's own workforce, across all operations in EU countries.

[S1-4 -38d, MDR-A-e] The action is planned to identify and evaluate the pay gaps and asses the possible scenarios to meet the legal requirements.

## Idea Management

[MDR-A-a, b, c] The main expected outcome is capturing, developing and rewarding valuable ideas from across the organi-zation that consistently support employees at all levels by using a dedicated tool. Rewards (bonuses) are paid for successfully implemented ideas, calculated based on a formula in case of quantifiable net benefits, while for non-quantifiable benefits (qualitative benefits), they are evaluated based on an estimation methodology. This action is continuous, and it covers OMV Petrom's own workforce.

[S1-4-38d, MDR-A-e] The progress of the Idea Management action is assessed qualitatively, 7 ideas uploaded in the system and assessed as of the beginning of 2024.

## Skills of the future

[MDR-A-a,b,c].The action is meant to help OMV Petrom to deliver its 2030 Low Carbon Business targets through staffing in all divisions by 2025. Short- to mid-term, LCB resources are secured mostly through a mixture of internal and external hires in order to ensure the right skillset and mindset. Mid to long-term, the company plans to secure capabilities through build up and upskilling of existing personnel and by leveraging existing partnerships, such as through fresh graduates from InnoEnergy programs and universities.

[MDR-A-b] covers OMV Petrom's own operations

## My SF Learning

The platform is continuously updated regarding Learning & Development (for personal, leadership, business, technical and digital skills), in accordance with business needs and the people development plans. The learning offer is available through the internal platform where employees can choose from a range of train-ing and development options fit to their position and career path.

[MDR-A-a, b, c] The main expected outcome is to build highly skilled and knowledgeable workforce. This action is continuous, and it covers OMV Petrom's afiliates' operations.

[S1-4-38d, MDR-A-e] The progress is tracked and communicated in the company's internal development tool, My Success Factor.

Speak Up channel

The Speak Up channel is a new platform for addressing work-related misconducts by employees, through a process that ensures neutrality and confidentiality of grievances addressed by employees. It is an extension of Integrity platform. The platform allows for the provision of remedy [MDR-A-d], and its main expected outcome is to assess reports and address them through specific processes. The action is continuous [MDR-A-c], and it covers OMV Petrom's own workforce and value chain [MDR-A-b].

[S1-4-39] To identify the actions needed and appropriate in response to actual or potential negative impacts on its employees, OMV Petrom engages with the employees to gain insight into potential negative impacts and understand the perspectives and concerns of its employees. These consultations take place through various communication channels, as for instance the periodical Health and Safety committees, open dialogue during Walk Arounds and the HSE audits.

The General HSSE framework is designed to identify and respond to negative health and safety impacts on the employees. It outlines targets, action plans, periodical monitoring, reporting and accommodates top-level managerial input.

Annually, the company develops an HSSE plan which follows local guidelines, legislative requirements, and global best practices, informed by strategic focus areas and initiatives. To ensure the plan's effectiveness, OMV Petrom conducts quarterly monitoring reviews to assess its implementation. The results are presented and analyzed by

the management representatives at different organizational levels, guiding the company's actions to reduce the actual or potential negative impacts.

In response to the health profiles of OMV Petrom's employees, the company designed and implemented health services, programs, and campaigns that target and address major health concerns. These initiatives aim to increase awareness, promote health, and prevent health complications, thereby mitigating actual or potential negative impacts on the well-being of its workforce.

<!-- Report Page 164 -->
[S1-4-40, S1-4-40a] The risk of injuries (both high and low consequence) is linked to the dependency that the company has on its workforce, namely, on its employees' commitment and productivity. In order to mitigate any Health and Safety risk, as part of HSSE Risk Management Standard implementation, occupational health and safety hazards are promptly identified, assessed, and control measures are implemented as per the standard guide-lines. These guidelines, derived from international standards set by IOGP/IPIECA, aim to assess various risks including chemical exposures, psychological strain, and physical injuries. The medical team collaboratively works with the local HSSE team to develop and maintain comprehensive and up-to-date occupational risks records at each site.

[S1-4-40b] Refer to the list of actions above for the actions planned or taken by OMV Petrom to pursue material opportunities for its own employees.

[S1-4-41] The company prioritizes the prevention and mitigation of material negative impacts on its workforce. The personnel policy centres on long-term employment that benefits both employees and the organization. OMV Petrom acknowledges the significance of job security for individuals, society, and the region, and it proactively manages responsibilities through contingency planning. In situations where changes in the business, organization, or security necessitate workplace adjustments or terminations, the company thoroughly explores all possibilities, engages in meaningful dialogue, and acts with consideration.

In cases where a transfer of parts of the undertaking occurs, the company ensures, under the social dialogue frame, that the existing rights and obligations are maintained with the new employer, providing a period of protection that offers predictability and security in their employment relationships.

In the area of Health and Safety, OMV Petrom's aim is to reach exceptional performance without incidents and impacts, by utilizing a strong and reliable HSSE management system. The primary guidance is deter-mined

by the company's Vision, Mission, Policies and Strategy. Specific regulations and process descriptions outline the functionality and scope of the management system; for more details, refer to the Policies section of this chapter. All OMV Petrom employees receive regular HSSE training pertinent to their roles and responsibilities to prevent negative impacts. HSSE training subjects are coordinated either at the global (OMV Group) or local (Company) level. They are organized through My Success Factors platform under the Learning module. In addition, within each business unit, local internal deliveries are carried out, tailored to legal requirements related to job exposure and frequencies implied within the specific activities.

[S1-4-43] Information regarding the resources allocated to OMV Petrom's action plan was included in the description of the actions, if relevant.

[S1-4-AR-40, S1-4-AR-40a] OMV Petrom's processes, initiatives and regulations, whose primary aim is to deliver positive impacts for its own workforce, are built upon continuous feedback from various levels of the organization and from all divisions, ensuring co-creation and increased engagement. Executive Board members are engaged not only in the approval process, but also in the communication throughout the organization, with key topics being disseminated via webcasts, events, and Intranet communication.

New processes and initiatives are discussed with employees' representatives, allowing the company to gain insights from all categories of employees and business areas. Working conditions aspects, secure employment, minimum wages, and benefits are subject to CLA negotiations, reflecting a consensus between the company and employees' representatives, namely the representative trade union.

[S1-4-AR-43] OMV Petrom undertakes efforts to mitigate the impact on its own workforce during the transition to a greener, climate-neutral economy. The company has undergone a complex transformation process, and has thus developed expertise in handling difficult situations, in the context of a just transition (such as restructurings and outsourcings). The company analyses and coordinates all such measures with social partners to reduce negative impacts on employees and to identify the best solutions for supporting their transition.

In this respect, trade unions are engaged early in the process to identify vulnerable categories, explore available solutions, and reduce the negative impact of the measures (through intra-company placements, reskilling, upskilling). In

compliance with the legislation and collective labour agreement, OMV Petrom provides protection for vulnerable categories, offers time off for affected employees for job searching, gives prior notice and financial compensations, as well as administrative assistance to facilitate access to public unemployment services.

## Achieve zero work-related fatalities

[MDR-T-a, b, c, e] The target to achieve zero work-related fatalities for 2030 advances OMV Petrom's commitment to work towards zero incidents. The target is directed linked to the HSSE policy, as the company's ambition is to become an industry leader in HSSE. The "Committed to Zero Harm - Protect People, Environment and Assets" vision guides its HSSE culture, behaviours, actions and decisions. The target is absolute, and covers separately employees, contractors' employees and third parties.

OMV Petrom considers as fatality the death of its own or contractor's employee, resulting from a work-related injury, when the person concerned dies within 12 months as a result of the injury. Fatality of a third party is the death of someone other than an employee, contractor or subcontractor, deathly injured or killed in conjunction with an operational activity of the company (for example, a driver hitting and killing an uninvolved third party such as a pedestrian). Non-work-related fatalities are not considered, and 'delayed' deaths (that occur after the incident) are included if they were a direct result of the incident [MDR-T-f].

[MDR-T- i, h, j] Internal stakeholders were involved in setting the target. No changes occurred in the target or the methodologies / assumptions behind it since the last reporting period. The progress is disclosed annually, and the achievement is monitored on a quarterly basis.

Increase average number of annual learning hours to a minimum of 30 hours per employee

[MDR-T-a, b, c, d, e, f] This target is directly linked to OMV Petrom's learning strategy 2024. The target is absolute and applies to all employees of the company. The target is not measured against a base year, however, it was set for 2030. The learning reports from the My Success Factor system, monitored through the Learning Dashboard, were used in establishing the target.

[MDR-T-h, i, j] The main stakeholder involved in setting the target is the Executive Board, as part of its decisional role on setting sustainability-related KPIs. No changes occurred in the target or the methodologies / assumptions behind it since the last reporting period. Learning hours are monitored via the Learning Dashboard.

Train all OMV Petrom Group employees in human rights

[MDR-T-a] This target is directly linked to OMV Petrom's Human Rights Policy Statement. [MDR-T-b, c] The target is relative and applies to all OMV Petrom Group employees. [MDR-T-d, e] The target is not measured against a base year, however, it was set for 2030. [MDR-T-f] The target is monitored throughout an internal database and progress is annually reported since 2022. [MDR-T-h] Internal stakeholders were involved in setting the target. [MDR-T-i] No changes occurred in the target or the methodologies/ assumptions behind it since the last reporting period. [MDRT-j] The progress is disclosed annually and the achievement is monitored on a quarterly basis.

Increase share of women at management level at 35% by 2030

[MDR-T-a, b, c, d, e, f, h, j] This target is directly linked to material IROs (job opportunities for all employees and development opportunities) and sustains our aims on diversity and inclusion area. The target applies to management positions at senior level (executive & advanced level). The target is not measured against a base year, however, it was set for 2030. The target is monitored based on reports via My SF system. The main stakeholder involved in setting the target is the Executive Board, as part of its decisional role on setting sustainability-related KPIs. No changes occurred in the target or the methodologies / assumptions behind it since the last reporting period.

## Maintain level of engagement:

[MDR-T 80a] Measuring the level of employees motivation contributes to achieving our business strategic objectives,

checking and action towards the indicators which directly impacts their engagement. [MDR-T 80b] A specific target level has not been established, aiming to maintain a satisfactory scores of engagement, results being interpreted considering the context. [MDR-T 80c] This target includes all own employees at consolidated group level. [MDR-T 80d] The baseline value and year is the previous Pulse Check year. [MDR-T 80e] We will measure Pulse Check and other engagement surveys until 2030. [MDR-T 80f, i] Pulse check is implemented via an external partner (Kincentric/Mercer) and contains questions aligned at group level, based on a standard understanding using international similar industry and top quartile organization benchmarks. Company history data is used for comparison. [MDR-T 80h] The Pulse check questionnaire is aligned with EB members. [MDR-T 80j] Pulse Check results are disseminated via the organization to Executive Board Members and all line managers in target to access results. P&C is owner of the initiative.

For OMV Petrom Group, excluding OMV Petrom Global Solutions S.R.L., in 2024, number of hours worked are 15 mn, LTIR is 0.34 and TRIR is 0.48.

Number of hours worked: [MDR-M 77a,c] The total number of hours performed and recorded in the company's internal reporting tool, Synergi. Absentees for longer periods (e.g. maternity leave) shall be excluded from this statistic. [MDRM 77b] The metric is not validated by an external body. [MDR-M 77c] The metric is labeled as Hours worked in the company's internal tool.

Number of fatalities as result of work related injuries: [MDRM 77a,c] A fatality is the death resulting from a work-related injury when the person concerned dies within 12 months as a result of the injury. Non work-related fatalities are not included. [MDR-M 77b] The metric is not validated by an external body.

Lost-Time Injury Rate: [MDR-M 77a,c] The number of lost time injuries (fatalities and lost workday injuries) per one million hours worked. The rate is calculated in the company's internal reporting tool, Synergi. Calculation and report: LTIR = LTI / hours worked * 1,000,000 (where LTI =

FAT + LWDI) LTIR is calculated on an annual basis. [MDRM 77b] The metric is not validated by an external body.

Number of recordable work-related accidents: [MDR-M 77 a,c] Total Recordable Injuries (TRI) is the sum of injuries resulting in fatalities, permanent total disabilities, lost workday injuries, restricted work injuries and medical treatment injuries. Data is reported and calculated in the company's internal reporting tool, Synergi. Calculation and report: TRI =FAT + LWDI + RWI + MTI. [MDR-M 77b] The metric is not validated by an external body.

Total Recordable Injury Rate: [MDR-M 77a,c] Total Recordable Injury Rate (TRIR) is the number of recordable injuries (fatalities + lost workday cases + restricted work day cases + medical treatment cases) per 1,000,000 hours worked. It describes the average incident frequency related to the working time performed. The metric is recorded in the company's internal reporting tool, Synergi. Calculation and report: TRIR = (FAT+LWDI+RWI+MTI) / hours worked * 1,000,000. Note: TRIR is calculated on an annual basis. [MDR-M 77b] The metric is not validated by an external body.

Number of days lost to work related injuries. [MDR-M 77a,c] Lost workdays, as labeled in the company's internal reporting tool, Synergi, represents the sum total of calendar days (i.e. running days including rest days, weekend days, leave days, public holidays) after the day on which the occupational injury occurred, where a person reported under LTI was unfit for work and did not work (synonym: days off work). Where absence from work extends beyond the year end, the actual or estimated days unfit for work in the following year should be added to those for the reporting year in computing the number of lost work days i.e. days unfit for work (only possible until the final close of the corresponding reporting period). The maximum lost workdays reportable for each lost workday case is 180. Days unfit for work between a fatal incident and the date of death are not included. Lost workdays resulting from commuting accidents are recorded separately. [MDR-M 77b] The metric is not validated by an external body.

## [DR S1-16] Remuneration metrics (total remuneration)

[S1-16.97b] The annual total renumeration ratio of the highest paid individual to the median annual total remuneration for all employees is 60.7.

[MDR-M-77.a] Data is reported from the SAP HR payroll system for Romania, while other countries provide their input via an email through local HR teams. All data are consolidated. An insignificant (<1%) number of employees with a different compensation policy was excluded, with no influence on the rate. The annual total remuneration ratio of the highest-paid individual to the median for all other employees is calculated using the formula: (Annual total remuneration for the highest paid individual) / (Median employee annual total remuneration [excluding the highestpaid individual]).

[MDR-M-77.b] The measurement of this metric is not validated by an external body other than the assurance provider.

## Workers in the value chain

To mitigate supply chain risks, OMV Petrom extends the legal requirements, internal rules, and standards applicable to OMV Petrom to its suppliers and contractors. Our supply chain partners are obligated to fully comply with the content of the OMV Petrom Code of Conduct and are required to sign the OMV Petrom Code of Conduct. In addition, our suppliers must accept the General Conditions of Purchase which further detail our business standards (e.g. labor rights), as an integral part of our contractual agreements. OMV Petrom reserves the right to terminate relationships with supply chain partners if noncompliance with applicable policies is identified or if is not rectified in a timely manner.

Safety carries paramount importance and is a fundamental component of OMV Petrom's processes. We hold the safety of our suppliers and contractors in the same high regard as that of our employees'. As such, we expect them to adhere to our standards and we monitor compliance throughout the entire duration of their contracts.

Furthermore, we have integrated sustainability requirements throughout our supply chain with the aim to drive a positive change in the sustainability performance of our suppliers and contractors while mitigating potential negative impacts such as economic disruptions due to delays in payment.

### Strategy

#### [SBM-3 S2] Material impacts, risks and opportunities and their interaction with strategy and business model

[S2-SBM 3.11a] For its double materiality assessment, OMV Petrom has performed a value chain mapping, which is presented in the General disclosures chapter. This showcases the location of the company's key material sustainability impacts, risks and opportunities across its full value chain.

The following workers materially impacted by OMV Petrom Group are included in the scope of disclosure under ESRS 2, as per the following categories:

Upstream:

Services: Workers of contractors (Tier 1) and subcontractors (Tier n) performing services at OMV Petrom Group's site or on behalf of OMV Petrom Group, e.g. drilling, road, water and air transportation, maintenance, engineering, facility management, catering, security, consultants working from their own offices, etc.

Products: Workers of Tier 1 supplier delivering goods/materials to OMV Petrom Group (compressors, raw materials, pipes, engines, etc.)

A contractor, selected in a tendering process based on its offer, provides services or works for OMV Petrom Group's companies, across various business areas and project phases, as defined in the contract.

A subcontractor is an entity, engaged by the Contractor to perform services / work packages that are part of a contract concluded between the Contractor and the Company (i.e., any OMV Petrom Group company) for the fulfilment of the contracted scope of work without being a party to this Contract. The Subcontractor is proposed by the Contractor

and approved by the Beneficiary after alignment with the Procurement Responsible prior contract awarding. Any new subcontractor proposed by Contractor during contract implementation has to be approved by Beneficiary in the Subcontractor Management application.

A supplier is a third-party company delivering goods or materials to OMV Petrom, but no services are associated to it.

[S2-SBM 3.11b] The company has not identified commodities, countries or geographic areas with a high risk from a human rights perspective for its value chain workers.

[S2-SBM 3.11c] For workers in the value chain, OMV Petrom has identified two potential negative impacts in relation to the working conditions subtopics:

Ineffective supplier engagement which may lead to poor labor practices or violation of human rights, is considered a limited impact as it is linked to individual relationships with each supplier or contractor.

Safety hazards associated with the activities of the upstream own operations, for contractors on our sites, which is considered a limited impact as it is linked to single operational sites.

[S2-SBM 3.11d] The following positive impacts have been identified for workers in the value chain:

Fair payment practices provide greater job security and better working conditions for upstream value chain workers.

OMV Petrom has implemented continuous monitoring of payment terms, to ensure compliance with the 60-day payment term through the SAP system, analysing and addressing any payment blocks.
<!-- Report Page 174 -->

Compliance with minimum human & labor rights for upstream value chain workers is ensured through OMV Petrom's policies (e.g. standards laid out in the OMV Petrom Code of Conduct are part of the General Purchasing Conditions). This impact is addressed through multiple actions, such as:

Supplier pre-qualification and adherence to the Code of Conduct to ensure that suppliers and contractors meet minimum human, labor rights, safety, and sustainability requirements as part of the pre-qualification and General Purchasing Conditions;

Improvement of subcontractor management through a new online approval process and tool to ensure transparency and compliance with OMV Petrom's HSSE, Compliance, and Human Rights requirements for subcontractors working on specific service contracts;

HSSE Walks with contractor managers to promote open dialogue with workers and their management and to ensure compliance.

Supplier engagement, pre-qualification, assessment and audits lead to improved working conditions for upstream value chain workers.

The actions undertaken by OMV Petrom to advance this positive impact include, additionally to the ones related to the previously listed positive impact, the following

Supplier audits and assessments, conducted onsite or off-site with external auditors or rating agencies, to evaluate suppliers' sustainability performance and mandate improvements for those scoring below 45 points in EcoVadis Assessments or requiring corrective actions from other audits;

Service quality and HSSE Experience exchange meetings with various stakeholders to enhance supplier engagement and assessment by identifying improvement opportunities, sharing good practices, and monitoring contractual KPIs to ensure continuous improvement and effective performance measurement;

Forums and safety performance meetings with contractors to enhance supplier engagement and assessment, aiming to identify gaps and improvements;

Road Safety Audits, incorporating the latest safety requirements and certifications, along with the annual Road Safety Forum, to enhance supplier engagement and assessment by

exchanging experiences, initiatives, best practices, and addressing transportation safety challenges.

For more information on each action, refer to section DR S2- 4 Actions under this chapter.

The categories of value chain workers that are positively affected are reported at S2-SBM 3.11 a.

[S2-SBM 3.11e] In line with the double materiality assessment process (please see to ESRS 2 - IRO-1), no material risks and opportunities resulted as material in relation to workers in the value chain.

[S2-SBM 3.12] OMV Petrom has identified the following vulnerable groups, with the potential to be materially impacted as part of the company's value chain workers:

Migrant workers

People with special needs

Minorities

Women

Young and elderly workers

Workers in Hazardous Roles

The methodology for identifying risks related to vulnerable categories includes employee surveys and data analysis such as: supplier risk report and monitoring and the company's incident reporting system Synergi.
