Monitoring and continuous improvement are achieved through regular assessments such as workplace audits, supplier and contractor audits and employee feedback.

##### Impact, risk and opportunity management [DR S2-1] Policies related to value chain workers

[S2-1.16] OMV Petrom has the following policies in place to address material impacts related to workers in the value chain:

##### Code of Conduct

[MDR-P-65a] Human rights are universal and guide the company's conduct in every aspect of OMV Petrom Group's activities. Human rights are inalienable, and every person is inherently entitled to them. OMV Petrom is committed to respecting the human rights of all its stakeholders, including but not limited to employees, business partners, local communities, and consumers. The company strives to be one of the best companies to work for and to be a fair and responsible employer that promotes equal opportunities,

modern forms of employee development, and a positive and dynamic working environment. The company is committed to respecting human rights in its activities, direct surroundings, and in the wider society. The acceptance of OMV Petrom's Code of Conduct is a mandatory part of supplier prequalification and the General Purchasing Conditions. Through the implementation of its Code of Conduct, OMV Petrom aims to enhance its actual positive impacts related to fair payment practices, compliance with minimum human & labour rights and supplier engagement, pre-qualification, assessment and audits, and to prevent its potential negative impacts related to ineffectiveness in supplier engagement, pre-qualification, assessment, audits and safety hazards

[MDR-P-65 b-f] For more information, please see the Climate change chapter.

## Corporate Procurement Directive

[MDR-P-65a] The Directive outlines the key processes for supplier engagement and management, embedding human rights considerations in supplier pre-qualification, audits, and meetings. Through this Directive, OMV Petrom aims to enhance its actual positive impacts related to fair payment practices, compliance with minimum human & labour rights and supplier engagement, pre-qualification, assessment and audits, and to prevent its potential negative impacts related to ineffectiveness in supplier engagement, pre-qualification, assessment and audits. Biannual checks are implemented by Procurement Units "Governance and Analytics" and "Strategy and Digitalization", to monitor the implementation of the Procurement Directive.

[MDR-P-65 b-f] For more information, please refer to the G1 Business conduct chapter.

## Personnel Transportation Standard

[MDR-P-65a, b, c]. This regulation defines the minimum requirements to manage transportation of OMV personnel and contractors, including transportation by road, rail, air and marine. The purpose of the Standard is prevention of transportation incidents, injuries, fatalities and resulting financial costs to the company by defining minimum requirements and providing guidance on how to implement transportation safety best practice, in each operational area within the scope of the OMV Petrom safety management system in accordance with international and national law. Bridging documents are used to transfer the requirements to the company's contractors. Line managers are accountable for the implementation of this standard. The standard is communicated through internal channels such as the internal regulations platform, included in the training, and

during the tendering process as part of the Minimum HSSE Requirements and Bridging Document in the contract execution phase [MDR-P-f].

## Contractor HSSE Management Standard

[MDR-P-65a]. The standard sets the minimum requirements for integrating HSSE aspects throughout all phases of contract and contractor management. It outlines the roles and responsibilities in managing contractor HSSE performance, ensuring a systematic approach beyond local and national regulations. The goal is to engage contractors capable of managing HSSE risks effectively. The assessment's depth corresponds to the HSSE risk level of the contracted activities. Through the standard, the company aims to enhance its actual positive impacts related to compliance with minimum human & labour rights and supplier engagement, pre-qualification, assessment and audits, and to prevent its potential negative impacts related to ineffectiveness in supplier engagement, pre-qualification, assessment, audits and safety hazards.

[MDR-P-b, c]This standard applies to all contracted field service and capital project activities within OMV Petrom Group's business divisions and corporate functions, regardless of the procurement process type. It is primarily aimed at high and medium-risk activities but can also be applied to low-risk activities as deemed necessary. Exclusions include office-based consultation activities, material suppliers (unless assessed with high HSSE risk potential), and the chartering of barges and vessels, which are managed according to international standards. The Contract Owner is accountable for the HSSE performance of the contract and serves as the escalation point for unresolved supplier HSSE issues.

The standard is communicated through internal channels such as the internal regulations platform, including training, and during the tendering process as part of the Minimum HSSE Requirements and Bridging Document in the contract execution phase [MDR-P-f].

[S2-1.17a] OMV Petrom commits to upholding the highest standards of human rights and labour rights for all workers in its value chain, ensuring fair wages, safe working conditions, and the right to collective bargaining. The company has a Human Rights Due Diligence Process in place to identify human rights-related potential impacts, monitor the effectiveness of the actions in this area, and integrate and communicate the findings from this process.

[S2-1.17b] Engagement with workers in the value chain occurs in all stages of our business relationship and at all

levels of stakeholders (both management and on-site workers).

Some examples of this engagement are:

Supplier audits and assessments
<!-- Report Page 176 -->

Service Quality meetings

HSSE Safety Walks

HSSE Audits

Human Rights spot checks

Annual meetings with strategic suppliers and sustainability supplier day events.

[S2-1.17c] In line with its double materiality process (please refer to ESRS 2 - IRO-1) and the results of the assessment for the topic related to Workers in the value chain (please refer to S2 Material IRO table), OMV Petrom has not identified any material negative impacts related to human rights of workers in the value chain.

Nevertheless, the company updated its Human Rights policy following feedback from peer reviews, benchmarking agencies, ESG rating agencies, reporting standards and upcoming legal requirements. Key updates include the commitment to provide remedy to correct negative impact if identified. OMV Petrom regards grievance mechanisms as essential tools for preventing and managing negative impacts on local communities, employees, and other stakeholders, such as value chain workers. The company's Human Rights Due Diligence Process involves four key steps:

Evaluating both current and potential human rights impacts linked to OMV Petrom's present and future business operations

Integrating and acting upon the findings in the company's activities

Monitoring the effectiveness of the company's actions

Communicating on how these impacts are being addressed

[S2-1.18] OMV Petrom strongly opposes forced labor, slavery, child labor and human trafficking. To ensure its suppliers' support of OMV Petrom's human rights values and to mitigate the risk of forced labor, slavery and human trafficking, the company's supply chain partners have to sign OMV Petrom's Code of Conduct including the provisions on human rights as part of their contract.

OMV Petrom reserves the right to terminate the relationships with its suppliers, if issues of non-compliance with OMV Petrom's Code of Conduct are discovered or noncompliance is not addressed in a timely manner.

[S2-1.AR-15] Contractors' HSSE management is regulated internally by the Group Standard Contractor HSSE Management. The Standard describes the HSSE management process throughout a contract's lifecycle and it is developed according to the best practice in Oil and Gas industry, specifically the IOGP Report 423 - "HSE management guidelines for working together in a contract environment". Also, contractors are requested to enforce similar HSSE selection and management protocols to their subcontractors and to support in this way the creation of a common HSSE culture among short- and long-term partners.

To mitigate supply chain risks, including forced labour, slavery, human trafficking, and corruption, OMV Petrom extends the legal requirements, internal rules, and standards applicable internally to its suppliers. They are required to fully comply with the content of the OMV Petrom Code of Conduct, and the company's supply chain partners are required to sign the OMV Petrom Code of Conduct. In addition, suppliers must accept the General Conditions of Purchase, which further detail OMV Petrom's business standards (e.g., labour rights), as an integral part of its contractual agreements. The Code of Conduct is fully in line with the applicable ILO standards.

[S2-1.19] The company updated its Human Rights policy following feedback from peer reviews, benchmarking agencies, ESG rating agencies, reporting standards and upcoming legal requirements.

OMV Petrom respects the Universal Declaration of Human Rights and international treaties like the International Labor Organization (ILO) core treaties (e.g., Freedom of Association and Protection of the Right to Organise Convention No. 87, Right to Organise and Collective Bargaining Convention No. 98, Forced Labour Convention No. 29, Abolition of Forced Labour Convention No. 105, Minimum Age Convention No. 138, Worst Forms of Child Labour Convention No. 182, Equal Remuneration Convention No. 100, Discrimination (Employment and Occupation) Convention, No. 111). Having signed the UN Global Compact, the company is fully committed to the UN Guiding Principles on Business and Human Rights and the OECD Guidelines for Multinational Enterprises.

In 2024 there were 0 cases of non-respect to international standards reported in OMV Petrom's value chain, involving issues such as human rights or labour rights violations.

[S2-1.AR-12] In 2024, OMV Petrom's Code of Conduct was updated to incorporate additional values and commitments. These changes aim to enhance accountability for both the

organization and its business partners in implementing responsible and sustainable business practices, as they will allow OMV Petrom to meet new expectations from suppliers and integrate recent legislative changes into its internal policies.

## [DR S2-2] Processes for engaging with value chain workers about impacts

[S2-2.22, S2-2.22a] OMV Petrom regularly engages with value chain workers to gain insight into their perspectives.
<!-- Report Page 177 -->

[S2-2.22b] The company engages with these stakeholders as follows:

Annually, through supplier audits and assessments;

Quarterly, through Service Quality meetings;

Annually, through forums and safety performance meetings with contractors;

Annually, through HSSE Walks with contractors;

Annually, through meetings with strategic suppliers and Sustainability Supplier Day events.

[S2-2.22c] The Head of HSSE (reporting directly to the CEO) and the Head of Procurement (reporting directly to the CFO) are the most senior roles within OMV Petrom that have operational responsibility for ensuring that engagement with value chain workers happens.

[S2-2.22d] OMV Petrom does not have Global Framework Agreements with global union federation with regards to value chain workers.

[S2-2.22e] OMV Petrom leverages on its 360 degrees evaluation, which is completed both by Petrom representatives and by the supplier, to understand if the regularity and the format of the supplier engagement methods that are used by OMV Petrom meet the expected requirements. The 2024 results of the 360 degree evaluations showed a generally widespread satisfaction regarding the engagement methods.

[S2-2.23] OMV Petrom gains insight into the perspective of workers that may be particularly vulnerable by identifying vulnerable groups and having in place feedback mechanisms through its grievance channels or Together for Sustainability (TfS) audits.

The company also undertakes HSSE Audits, including Human Rights specific evaluations, and on-site verifications for working conditions and compliance with the Law.

## [DR S2-3] Processes to remediate negative impacts and channels for value chain workers to raise concerns

[S2-3.27a] The key tools for preventing and managing adverse impacts on OMV Petrom's stakeholders, including workers in the value chain, are the grievance mechanisms. Following the UN Effectiveness Criteria, the company aims to address all grievances received within 45 days of receipt, regardless of whether they stem from real or perceived issues and whether the complainant is identified or anonymous.

The company's mechanisms are designed to provide a comprehensive channel for identifying potential adverse impacts, resolving grievances, and offering remedies to rightsholders in instances where the company has caused or contributed to negative outcomes. OMV Petrom recognizes that does not hinder the internal and external stakeholders' (e.g. value chain workers) right to access judicial or other remedies.

Through the grievance mechanism, the company tracks and monitors issues raised. The company measures the number of grievances resolved through remediation, as well as the number of resolved grievances against the number of received ones. For more information regarding ensuring the effectiveness of this channel, refer to chapter Affected communities.

A grievance is defined as an expression of dissatisfaction stemming from a real or perceived impact of the company's business activities, communicated verbally or in writing, including local languages. They can be lodged by email, phone, through our community relations staff working locally and other locally dedicated channels, as well as at a corporate level. The grievances can be expressed anonymously, as well as on behalf of another individual. OMV Petrom does not seek retaliation against any stakeholder who lodges a grievance.

[S2-3.27b] In addition to the grievance mechanism through which workers in the value chain can raise their concerns, OMV Petrom performs regular TfS sustainability audits and assessments, as well as on-site HSSE compliance checks and audits.

For any issues related to corruption and bribery, cartels/unfair competition, capital market law, public procurement, environmental protection, product/food safety and consumer protection, corporate tax regulations, data protection, and for any other breaches of the Romanian law, workplace concerns, any stakeholder, including workers in the value chain, can submit a complaint via the anonymous

and confidential Integrity Platform. The platform is available to both OMV Petrom employees and external parties.

[S2-3.27c] OMV Petrom Group ensures grievance channels are available within its direct operations and through business relationships, by mandating grievance management as a requirement in contracts with partners, suppliers and contractors.

[S2-3.27d] The grievance mechanisms ensure the systematic receipt, investigation, documentation, addressing, and resolution of grievances across all operating countries in a timely, fair, and consistent manner, establishing the foundation for its social license to operate.

All grievances are promptly responded to within a specified time period, with complainants informed about the procedure, indicative time frame for each stage and progress.
<!-- Report Page 178 -->

The management of grievances aligns with the IPIECA best practice guidelines and with the Effectiveness Criteria of the UN Guiding Principles on Business and Human Rights, aiming for legitimacy, accessibility predictability, equity, transparency, rights-compatibility, continuous learning, and engagement and dialogue. OMV Petrom has set a target to assess the grievance mechanisms at all sites against the UN Effectiveness Criteria for Non-Judicial Grievance Mechanisms by 2025.

For more information regarding tracking and monitoring of the issues raised and addressed, and ensuring the effectiveness of the grievance channel, refer to Affected communities chapter.

[S2-3.28] In 2024 there was no action to assess awareness of the grievance mechanisms of value chain workers; however, OMV Petrom is considering implementing this in the future.

[S2-3.28] Whistleblowers are subject to special protection against retaliation ("whistleblower protection") by OMV Petrom, as legally provided.

Retaliation comprises all actions or omissions in a workrelated context that are a reaction to a report and because of which the whistleblower suffers or may suffer an unjustified detriment. This includes, in particular, detriments with regard to professional advancement, further training, income, or other professional development opportunities, such as dismissal, demotion, denial of promotion, negative performance appraisal, or disciplinary measures.

OMV Petrom's internal Whistleblowing Directive stipulates the "no retaliation" policy aiming at protecting individuals who report misconducts in good faith and outline methods for employees and external workers to make confidential and anonymous whistleblower complaints.

[DR S2-4] Actions

[S2-4-31] OMV Petrom takes actions to address material negative and positive impacts related to workers in the value chain. The list of actions taken or planned by the company in this regard is presented below:

## Continuous monitoring of payment terms

[S2-4-32c, MDR-A-a,b,c] This action has been undertaken to advance the company's positive impact regarding fair payment practices. The continuous monitoring helps ensure that the 60 days payment term is not exceeded. This is achieved through the SAP system through automatic payment proposal, for all invoices that are due, one day prior to expiry of the payment term (thus avoiding manual intervention). In case there are invoices blocked for payment (e.g., for tax considerations), the reasons are analyzed and remediation measures are initiated. The monitoring is carried out on a monthly basis and covers OMV Petrom's contractors and suppliers.

[S2-4-32d, MDR-A-e] In terms of progress, in 2024, in OMV Petrom 93.2% of payments were aligned with the standard payment term of 60 days.

For more information regarding this action, please refer to the G1 Business conduct chapter.

Supplier pre-qualification and adherence to Code of Conduct

[S2-4-32a, S2-4-32c, MDR-A-a] The acceptance of OMV Petrom's Code of Conduct is a mandatory part of prequalification and the General Purchasing Conditions. This action has been undertaken to advance the company's positive impacts regarding compliance with minimum human, labor rights & safety conditions and supplier engagement, pre-qualification, supplier assessment and audits and to prevent the negative impact related to ineffectiveness in supplier relationships management. The expected outcome is to ensure pre-qualification of those suppliers and contractors that meet the minimum HSSE and sustainability requirements.

[MDR-A-b, MDR-A-68c] The pre-qualification and acceptance of the Code of Conduct is mandatory for all

suppliers and contractors invited to tenders as of 2022. This action is undertaken continuously and progress on the implementation status is performed biannually through internal control system checks.

[S2-4-32d, MDR-A-68e] In terms of progress, in 2024, in OMV Petrom 112 new suppliers (vs. 212 in 2023) were screened for social criteria (e.g., child labor, forced labor, and collective bargaining) and environmental criteria. No suppliers were disqualified on environmental criteria, while 7% were disqualified on social criteria.

Run supplier assessments through the EcoVadis platform provided by TfS and on-site or off-site audits with TfS or other external auditors.

[S2-4-32a, S2-4-32c. MDR-A-68a, d] This action has been undertaken to advance the company's positive impacts regarding compliance with minimum human, labor rights & safety conditions and supplier engagement, pre-qualification, supplier assessments and audits and to prevent the negative impact related to ineffectiveness in supplier relationships management. The purpose of the external supplier audits is to assess their performance on different dimensions, including sustainability and identify areas of improvements. TfS assessments (performed via EcoVadis platform) address areas such as health and safety, labour and human rights and sustainable procurement practices. The EcoVadis assessments are run through an online questionnaire. If the suppliers score below 45 points, they are required to improve their performance within one year and repeat the assessment. For corrective action plans resulting from the audits, suppliers must agree on a timeline to implement the necessary corrective actions.

By 2025, OMV Petrom aims to conduct sustainability evaluations (EcoVadis) and TfS audits for all suppliers covering 80% of procurement spend and, in addition, perform at least 20 audits with an external auditor for selected suppliers and contractors. By 2030, the sustainability evaluations (EcoVadis) and TfS audits will be extended to all suppliers covering 90% of procurement spend [MDR-A-68c, MDR-A-68b]. The progress of the external supplier audits is monitored on a quarterly basis, as for the TfS audits and EcoVadis assessments on a monthly basis.

[S2-4-32d, MDR-A-e] In terms of progress, in 2024, in OMV Petrom there were 24 supplier audits performed with external auditors (vs 16 in 2023), 2 TfS audits, 130 EcoVadis (re)assessments (vs. 53 in 2023) and a total of 162 suppliers with valid EcoVadis scorecards (no more than 3 years old).

Improvement of subcontractor management with the implementation of a new online subcontractors' approval process (SAP BTP).
<!-- Report Page 179 -->

[S2-4-32a, S2-4-32c, MDR-A-68a] This action has been undertaken to advance the company's positive impacts regarding compliance with minimum human, labor rights & safety conditions and supplier engagement, pre-qualification, supplier assessments and audits and to prevent the negative impact related to ineffectiveness in supplier relationships management. The main expected outcome is to have a transparent overview of subcontractors approved to work for OMV Petrom for specific service contracts and to ensure the subcontractors meet OMV Petrom requirements in terms of HSSE, Compliance, Human Rights etc.

[MDR-A-b] Contractors have to submit all the subcontractors planned to be used for specific service contracts for approval in the SAP BTP application as of 2023. This action is undertaken continuously and checks are performed on a quarterly basis [MDR-A-c], and covers OMV Petrom's contractors and their subcontractors.

[S2-4-32d, MDR-A-68e] In terms of progress, in 2024, in OMV Petrom there were 550 subcontractors submitted for approval by our contractors via the SAP BTP database.

Service quality meetings and HSSE Experience exchange meetings with HSSE, Contract Holders, Contract Owners, beneficiaries and peer reviews

[S2-4-32a, S2-4-32c, MDR-A-68a] These meetings are held to prevent safety hazards associated with the activities of upstream operations, for contractors on the company's sites. The expected outcome of the HSSE Experience exchange meetings and peer reviews include enhanced identification of improvement opportunities through internal analysis, feedback, and sharing of good practices regarding safe ways of working, lessons learnt regarding actual and potential safety incidents. By working together on improvements and monitoring contractual KPIs, OMV Petrom aims to measure performance effectively, analyze incidents, and utilize bridging documents to ensure continuous improvement in the scope of works. The Service quality meeting is structured to include topics such as: follow up on HSSE walks and audits conclusions, updates on contractors' workers' trainings, competences and human rights, updates on subcontractors' workers and planning of employees' qualification.

The meetings are organized on a quarterly basis [MDR-A68c], and apply to contractors with HSSE relevance, which

includes those contractors with medium-high risk as per the Contractor Management Standard [MDR-A-68b].

[S2-4-32d, MDR-A-68e] In terms of progress, in 2024, OMV Petrom organized 15 Experience Exchange meetings.

Joint HSSE Walks with contractors' management representatives

[S2-4-32a, S2-4-32c, MDR-A-a] This action has been undertaken to advance the company's positive impacts regarding compliance with minimum human, labor rights & safety conditions. The main expected outcome is to promote open dialogue with workers in the value chain and their management.

[MDR-A-b] The HSSE walks are undertaken in line with the HSSE Annual Plan and outcomes are further discussed in service quality meetings [MDR-A-c], and apply to contractors with HSSE relevance, which include those contractors with medium-high risk as per the Contractor Management Standard.

[S2-4-32d, MDR-A-e] In terms of progress, in 2024, OMV Petrom 8900 HSSE Walks were performed.

## Contractors Road Safety

[S2-4-32a, S2-4-32c] OMV Petrom organizes Transportation audits which aim at preventing the negative impacts related to safety hazards associated with transportation of contractors to the company's sites. These audits include the latest road safety requirements, including IVMS for the entire fleet, safety cameras for drivers, and ISO 39001 certification for four R&M contractors/haulers from Bulgaria and Moldova.

[MDR-A-a] The company also organizes the annual Road Safety Forum, now in its third edition, to facilitate the exchange of experiences, initiatives, best practices, and challenges in transportation safety.

[MDR-A-b, c] The audits are planned annually, and cover OMV Petrom's contractors.

[S2-4-32d] In terms of progress, in 2024, OMV Petrom, 46 HSSE MS audits at contractors working on OMV Petrom site were performed [MDR-A-e].

Conduct HSE Contractors Onboarding

OMV Petrom aims to ensure the onboarding of key contractors and provide dedicated HSSE support during the

ramp-up phase, aligning with strategic development objectives [MDR-A-68a]. This action is forward-looking, with the company planning to implement it in 2025 [MDR-A-68c], and covers OMV Petrom's contractors [MDR-A-68b].

Forums with contractors

[S2-4-32a, S2-4-32c, MDR-A-a] OMV Petrom organizes regular forums with contractors aiming at sharing safety related day-to-days practices, challenges and lessons learnt, thus reinforcing the fundamental need of living the Life Saving Rules.
<!-- Report Page 180 -->

[MDR-A-b, c] The meetings are organized on a quarterly or bi-annual basis, and apply to contractors with HSSE relevance, which includes those contractors with mediumhigh risk as per the Contractor Management Standard.

[S2-4-32d] In terms of progress, in 2024, OMV Petrom performed 15 meetings with big contractors [MDR-A-e].

[S2-4-33a] In line with the double materiality assessment process (described in the General disclosures chapter, section IRO-1) and results related to Workers in the value chain, OMV Petrom identified two potential negative impacts on workers in the value chain.

The first potential negative impact is ineffective supplier engagement, pre-qualification, assessment, audits, Code of Conduct implementation which may lead to the risk of poor labour practices, as well as child labour, forced labour, human trafficking, or any other violation of human rights in the company's value chain.

For example, in the development of the Neptun Deep project, OMV Petrom is working with Saipem that will execute engineering, procurement, construction, installation and commissioning services (EPCIC). In this regard, human rights due diligence is a prudent step to identifying, preventing, mitigating potential impacts and risks to the workforce.

In 2024, as part of the pre-start assurance of the Saipem fabrications yards in Indonesia and Italy and the Saipem technology centre in Romania, we undertook on-site human rights spot-checks that focused on an increased attention to the labour rights, working conditions, health and safety in accordance with their human rights policy and codes of ethics and relevant national legislation. Key topics such as work authorisation, decent living wages, compulsory overtime, work hours, rest times, freedom and termination of employment, accommodation & living conditions, minimum age requirements, access to personal documents, collective

representation, trainings, whistleblowing channels were assessed and no significant concerns were identified.

The second potential negative impact is safety hazards associated with the activities of the upstream own operations, for contractors on OMV Petrom's sites, which has the potential to result in high-consequence work-related injuries.

OMV Petrom's Procurement department undertakes two types of audits that aim to measure and enhance supplier performance, so as to meet the company's requirements. These audits are carried out as part of the pre-awarding phase of the tender and/ or during contract execution. Onsite Together for Sustainability audits, focus on the sustainability performance of a company. Off-site audits performed with external auditors analyse areas such as the financial stability of suppliers, their strategy and organization, supply chain, sustainability matters or cybersecurity. Audit findings classified with a red flag are followed up and analyzed by the Procurement team in collaboration with business representatives and other relevant functions. Information on the outcome of the audit is made available to the supplier, and the supplier is requested to submit a proposed corrective plan with concrete measures and an implementation timeline. In 2024, 4 audits resulted in follow-up measures.

[S2-4-33b] The actions previously identified help OMV Petrom to prevent its potential negative impacts on value chain workers.

In terms of supplier engagement and capacity building, in response to the identified potential negative impact of ineffective supplier engagement, OMV Petrom works together with its suppliers to enhance their understanding and compliance with sustainability requirements. In 2024, individual meetings and webinars were offered to suppliers to help them better understand the climate change questionnaire and the TfS (Taskforce for Sustainability) assessment. These sessions emphasized the importance of sustainability information to OMV Petrom. Sustainable and low-carbon procurement topics were also included in the agenda of annual strategic supplier meetings. The TfS program, based on the principles of the UN Global Compact and Responsible Care®, helps OMV Petrom integrate sustainability into its daily operations and extend these requirements throughout our supply chain.

In line with the actions identified to mitigate safety hazards associated with upstream operations, OMV Petrom uses its HSSE Management System to verify contractors' performance against pre-award expectations, ensuring

compliance with legal and internal HSSE requirements. Post-audit, the company monitors the development of action plans and improvements and best practices to the eliminate risks to humans, the environment, and assets. Site visits, known as Walk Arounds or Inspections, are conducted together with on-site workers to analyse compliance with Life Saving Rules and the implementation of Permit to Work, identifying improvements collaboratively.

For the full list of actions undertaken by OMV Petrom in response to its negative impacts related to value chain workers and their expected outcomes, refer to the list of actions described above this chapter.

[S2-4-33c] OMV Petrom leverages on its grievance mechanisms to provide and enable remedy for negative impacts on its stakeholders, including workers in the value chain. The company tracks and monitors the number of grievances registered and resolved, including through remediation. As part of the Human Rights Due Diligence Process, the effectiveness and the progress of OMV Petrom's response to such issues are monitored and communicated.

For more information regarding the process to enable remedy and ensuring the effectiveness of the grievance channel, refer to Own workforce chapter.

[S2-4-34a, b] In line with the double materiality assessment process (described in the General disclosures chapter, section IRO-1) and results related to Workers in the value chain, OMV Petrom has not identified material risks and opportunities associated with Workers in the value chain.

[S2-4-35] Supplier prequalification is part of precontractual activities, during which OMV Petrom collects information from a potential supplier with the purpose of evaluating compliance with HSSE and sustainability requirements. The goal of the prequalification process is to screen potential suppliers before bringing them onboard to ensure that only those suppliers that meet HSSE and sustainability standards can be considered for future collaboration.

The prequalification is based on a standardized list of elements and objectives that aligns with the OMV Petrom HSSE Management System (e.g., HSSE Policy, ISO 9001, 14001, 45001) and sustainability objectives (e.g., Sustainability Policy, Human Rights Policy, and Grievance Mechanisms).

For more information regarding the audits performed by OMV Petrom with regards to its suppliers and contractors, refer to the "Actions" section within this chapter.

[S2-4-36] No severe human rights issues and incidents have been reported in the value chain in 2024.

[S2-4-38] Since 2022, the Sustainable Procurement and Supplier Innovation Department organizes webinars and training sessions with the aim to increase awareness on sustainability for the value chain workers. Also, when a supplier is invited to complete an EcoVadis assessment, it gains access to an e-learning platform containing a catalogue of courses on sustainable business practices (including labour practices and human rights). Furthermore, suppliers can also register to the TfS Academy platform, where a variety of courses are available on topics like discrimination and harassment, human trafficking, modern slavery, child labour, recognizing and preventing forced labour etc. OMV Petrom's own employees also receive training on value chain workers' rights through programs like mandatory Human Rights e-Learning or additional tailored classroom training modules.

<!-- Report Page 181 -->
In 2023, OMV Petrom introduced a new initiative for developing Life Saving Rules Training Centres. 6 out of 8 locations were finalized in 2024. In 2024, Following the "Train the trainers" principle, contractors' representatives received practical application and examples to ensure compliance with each of the nine OMV Petrom Life Saving Rules in order to further roll-out these sessions to the larger population of contractors' workers. This is part of our commitment to support our contractors in reaching similar levels of safety culture maturity.

## Metrics and targets [DR S2-5] Targets related to workers in the value chain

[S2-5-41] OMV Petrom has set targets to address material impacts on workers in the value chain. These are presented below:

All new suppliers screened against mandatory ESG criteria during supplier pre-qualification prior sourcing projects.

[MDR-T-80a] Supplier prequalification is part of precontractual activities during which OMV Petrom gathers information from a potential supplier in order to assess their compliance with our HSSE and other sustainability requirements. In line with the Corporate Procurement Directive and the Contractor HSSE Management Standard (Annex 2 HSSE Pre-Qualification), OMV Petrom aims to screen all new suppliers against mandatory ESG criteria during pre-qualification, so as to ensure that only those suppliers who meet our HSSE and Sustainability standards can be considered for further collaboration.

[MDR-T-80b, c] This is an absolute target, measured by the number of new suppliers screened in SAP during the prequalification phase, during the reporting period. It applies to the upstream value chain across the entire OMV Petrom Group.

[MDR-T-80d, e] The target was first introduced in 2022. No baseline value was set as this target applies yearly, and the target periods are set for 2030.

[MDR-T-80f] The prequalification process is based on a standardized list of elements and objectives that aligns with the OMV Petrom's HSSE Management System (e.g., HSSE Policy, ISO 9001, 14001, 45001) and our Sustainability Framework (e.g., Sustainability Policy, Human Rights Policy, and Grievance Mechanisms).

[MDR-T-80h, i] Only internal stakeholders were involved in defining this target. No changes in the target and corresponding metrics or underlying measurement methodologies, significant assumptions, limitations, sources and processes to collect data have been undertaken during the reporting period.

[MDR-T-80j] In 2024, in OMV Petrom 112 new suppliers were screened against mandatory ESG criteria (HSSE and sustainability requirements). The target is reviewed annually, to check the effectiveness of measures and make adjustments as needed. Progress for 2024 is in line with the target that was set.

Achieve zero work-related fatalities: This target covers own employees, contractors and third parties.

[MDR-T a-j]: For details on the target, please see Own workforce chapter.

[S2-5-42] OMV Petrom engaged both internal stakeholders (e.g., EB members, Sustainability sub-department) and external stakeholders (i.e., TfS organization, CDP organization) in the target-setting process to ensure the targets are realistic and aligned with their needs. The involvement was facilitated through various channels, including meetings and peers' analysis. The company periodically reviews and adjusts the targets if needed, documenting and reporting on the entire process to maintain transparency and trust.

Additionally, engagement for target-setting has been ensured through:

HSSE Walk arounds, audits and inspections, fostering open dialogue and encouraging workers to disclose their safety-related concerns
<!-- Report Page 182 -->

Quarterly meetings with key contractors under the Initiative Partners for a safer Future, aimed at improvement through common initiatives and working groups

Biannual HSSE forum with Contract Holders

Providing contractors access to Safety Centres, where practical LSR trainings are delivered to their employees

From these dialogues and communication channels, OMV Petrom has gained an understanding of the importance of proper onboarding for contractors to ensure a unified risk management approach. Consequently, this objective was incorporated into the HSSE Strategy, and efforts are underway to identify the proper KPIs for measurement.

[S2-5-42b] Performance against targets is monitored quarterly, within the Safety Taskforce meetings.

[S2-5-42c] Continuous performance monitoring in 2024 indicated no need for additional improvement of the performance targets.

## [ESRS 2 MDR-M] Metrics related to workers in the value chain

[ESRS 2 MDR-M.75] OMV Petrom uses the following metrics to evaluate performance and effectiveness in relation to its impacts on value chain workers, which are disclosed in relation to the targets presented above:

Metric 1: The Number of supplier audits with external auditor, through which the company evaluates its performance in relation to the impacts related to supplier engagement, pre-qualification, assessment and audits: 24 audits conducted.

Metric 2: TfS related metrics through which the company evaluates its performance in relation to the impacts related to suppliers' compliance with minimum human & labor rights standards, supplier engagement, pre-qualification, assessments and audits:

number of TfS (Re)Assessments performed via EcoVadis: 130

number of suppliers with a valid EcoVadis score (no more than 3 years old): 162

number of new suppliers screened for social criteria (e.g., child labor, forced labor, and collective bargaining) and environmental criteria: 112

number of TfS audits performed: 2

[ESRS 2 MDR-M.77a] For Metric 1, which encompasses the number of supplier audits performed with an external auditor, OMV Petrom collects the audit reports that are finalized on an ongoing basis. There is no calculation related to this metric, the number of audits performed with an external auditor is measured at the end of the year, with quarterly checks for implementations status.

Nota bene: An audit is considered completed once an audit report delivered by the external auditor is accepted by Procurement, and services delivered are invoiced.

For Metric 2, which refers to the TfS commitments, TfS audit reports are collected by OMV Petrom via OASIS platform, whereas the data pertaining to the metrics regarding TfS assessments is collected from Tableau platform (both platforms are provided by TfS). OMV Petrom receives a separate report from the TfS team and double checks if the information in Tableau and Oasis fits to the info received from TfS team. So far, no deviations were identified.

[ESRS 2 MDR-M.77b] For TfS metrics, the external validation body is TfS organization. For the other metrics there is no validation from an external body.

## Affected communities

At OMV Petrom, the commitment to transitioning to a lower carbon future is intertwined with a dedication to working closely with local communities to mitigate or prevent direct or indirect impacts of the company's operations, while maintaining our social license to operate. By prioritizing local issues, accounting for regional variances, and taking the necessary steps to guarantee long-term, sustainable development, OMV Petrom ensures that the path to a lower

carbon future is both environmentally and socially responsible. Furthermore, OMV Petrom aims to make a positive impact on communities by being open to their needs, responding to their concerns, and promoting their long-term progress. This approach not only fosters local support and collaboration but also aligns with the broader goal of achieving a sustainable and inclusive energy transition.

### Strategy

#### [SBM-3 S3] Material impacts, risks and opportunities and their interaction with strategy and business model

[S3-SBM 3.8] OMV Petrom recognizes that its strategy and business model could impact the communities where it operates. The company's social license to operate is founded on upholding human and labor rights and fostering positive relationships with surrounding communities.

The company acknowledges both positive and negative material impacts, which contribute to adapting the company's strategy. In particular, OMV Petrom strengthens its commitment to respecting and fulfilling human rights in its immediate surroundings as well as in the wider society,

while avoiding or mitigating any risks that may arise from project-related activities.

In addition to shaping OMV Petrom's strategic commitment towards communities, the company's impacts inform how each Business Division tailors its community engagement and initiatives, in line also with its business plans, so that specific local needs are addressed and the respect of human rights is upheld.

[S3-SBM 3.9a] The following communities are subject to OMV Petrom's material impacts:

Local communities (which includes residents in areas adjacent to OMV Petrom's sites, refinery and pipeline routes, directly impacted by the company's operations);

Communities alongside the company's upstream supply chain.

For an overview of OMV Petrom's value chain, showing where its key material sustainability impacts, risks and opportunities occur across the company's full value chain, please refer to the General disclosures chapter.

OMV Petrom has identified three material negative impacts on affected communities, displayed at the beginning of this chapter.

<!-- Report Page 185 -->
##### [S3-SBM 3.9b] Community dependence on OMV Petrom

OMV Petrom is aware of the dependence of local suppliers and community members on its operations and products. More specifically, social and economic impacts could include job loss and impacts on livelihoods, while a lack of relevant skills, knowledge, or accessible training programs could affect community economic health and overall quality of life. The implementation of OMV Petrom's Strategy 2030, with a strong commitment to the energy transition, will also bring a transformation to the jobs it offers, many of which employ people from the company's local communities. The impact is limited to the operational areas in which OMV Petrom operates, and from which the company employs members of the communities.

##### Noise and disturbance of surrounding communities

Due to the profile of the industry in which it operates, OMV Petrom's supply chain and logistics can impact local communities through traffic congestion, noise and disturbance for communities where it operates. The impact is widespread, given the fact that the company's suppliers operate over a large geographic area.

##### Process safety incidents

Process Safety Management (PSM) remains a crucial focus for the company. A process safety incident that would result in spills could impact communities' health, safety, quality of life and the environment). OMV Petrom is committed to mitigating this potential impact through prevention measures. The impact is related to potential individual incidents.

For more information regarding the company's Process Safety Management, please refer to the Process safety chapter.

[S3-SBM 3.9c] With regards to material positive impacts on affected communities, OMV Petrom has identified the following impact:

Creation of opportunities for communities around suppliers and own operations

OMV Petrom significantly contributes to the economic and social wellbeing of the communities through local employment, local taxes, other payments to local governments, local procurement and community development programs.

OMV Petrom's engagement in local and national initiatives across education, health, and environmental sectors results in significant positive impacts for various communities in Romania. Our community investment projects are prioritized, planned, and managed in partnership with local stakeholders and the budget is allocated yearly.

For more information, see the "Material impacts, risks and opportunities related to Affected Communities section of this chapter.

[S3-SBM 3.9d; 3.11] OMV Petrom has not identified material risks and opportunities arising from impacts and dependencies on affected communities, following its double materiality assessment.

The Community Relations and Community Development Handbook outlines the management processes that OMV Petrom Group entities must follow to maintain their social license to operate. Through comprehensive community stakeholder analysis, management can identify and understand the communities whose interests or rights are affected by, or who can influence the company's development and operational activities in specific projects or operations. This analysis acknowledges the interests of key community stakeholders, including vulnerable groups. OMV Petrom addresses the needs of vulnerable groups within targeted communities, such as women, children, people with special needs, minorities, and migrants. In alignment with the International Standards, OMV Petrom considers the groups previously listed as groups which could be in vulnerable situations, such as those that could be physically or economically isolated, or that have health vulnerabilities, or having limited access to social services and therefore rely on infrastructure set up by OMV Petrom.

[S3-SBM 3.10] The company collaborates with local and national NGOs through various social investment projects and sponsorships, to gain insights into the specific vulnerabilities of these groups. Additionally, OMV Petrom's

stakeholder dialogue and grievance mechanisms enable the collection of direct feedback from communities, ensuring that the concerns of all members, including vulnerable groups, are heard and addressed effectively.

For more details, please refer to the section Actions of this chapter.

# Impact, Risk and Opportunity Management [DR S3-1] Policies

[S3-1.14] OMV Petrom has in place the following policies to address material impacts on affected communities:

## Code of Conduct

[MDR-P-65a] According to our Code of Conduct, the nature of our operations means that we have an impact on the local communities in which we operate. Our aim is to establish and maintain good relations with our stakeholders. Many of our business activities are visible to local people and to some extent will intrude on their communities. OMV Petrom implements community development projects that contribute to the local needs and UN SDGs. OMV Petrom values the contribution of the employees who support them on a voluntary base. In 2024, we updated our Code of Conduct and included more specific commitments regarding the impacts on communities, stemming for run responsible operations (e.g. we are committed to implementing loss prevention and control measures to avoid water and soil pollution, by following regulatory frameworks and industry best practice. Any spill shall be cleaned up promptly and immediate assessment shall be done to prevent further occurrence, foster people and communities (e.g. we aim to build capacity in order to support local development.).

[MDR-P 65b-f] For more details, please refer to the E1 Climate change chapter.

<!-- Report Page 186 -->
## Social Investments Standard

[MDR-P 65a, b, c, d] The Social Investments Standard is aimed at advancing the company's positive impact related to creating opportunities for communities around suppliers and own operations, and at preventing the negative impacts on community dependence on OMV Petrom. The Social Investments Standard ensures transparency in the effective use of financial resources allocated for sponsorships, both monetary and in-kind. It provides clear guidance over the process through which OMV Petrom gets involved with the communities where it currently operates, plans to develop or intends to conduct future activities. The Social Investments Standard's implementation is overseen by the Head of

Communication & Sustainability, and through its implementation, the company is committed to respect the United Nations' Sustainable Development Goals. This standard applies OMV Petrom SA and its fully consolidated subsidiaries as well as OMV Petrom Global Solutions S.R.L.

[MDR-P-65e, f] OMV Petrom engages with relevant internal stakeholder groups in order to understand their expectations in relation to the company when defining policies. The Social Investment Standard is available on the company's internal regulatory platform.

## Community Grievance Mechanism

[MDR-P-65a] The mechanism is aimed at advancing the company's positive impact related to creating opportunities for communities around suppliers and own operations, and at preventing the negative impacts on community dependence on OMV Petrom and noise and disturbance of surrounding communities. The Grievance Management Process at OMV Petrom Group is integrated in the Compliance Management System, which ensures compliance with applicable legal provisions and internal regulations. This aims to increase the efficiency and effectiveness of our dialogue with stakeholders and communities in particular and to reduce non-compliance risks and financial consequences for the company.

[MDR-P 65b]The Community Grievance Mechanism is applicable across the OMV Petrom Group and its consolidated subsidiaries, including the associated company, OMV Petrom Global Solutions SRL and all their employees

[MDR-P 65c] The Head of Compliance oversees the implementation of the Compliance Management System and, implicitly, of the Community Grievance Mechanism.

[MDR-P 65d] Through the mechanism, OMV Petrom aims to respect the United Nations Guiding Principles on Business and Human Rights.

[MDR-P-65e]. The process for setting up the system is informed by multiple internal stakeholders and their expectations in relation to the company.

[MDR-P-65f] The Community Grievance Mechanism Standard is accessible through the company's internal regulatory platform, while details about channels are available on our corporate website.

[S3-1.15, S3-2.23] OMV Petrom Group does not operate in areas with indigenous populations; as such, the company

does not have impacts on indigenous peoples and has no particular policy provisions for preventing and addressing such impacts, and does not have a stakeholder approach regarding these populations.

### Human Rights Policy Statement

[MDR-P-a] OMV Petrom's Human Rights Policy Statement outlines the company's commitment to upholding human rights across its business operations and supply chain. The Policy is aimed at advancing the company's positive impact related to creating opportunities for communities around suppliers and own operations, and at preventing the negative impacts of community dependence on OMV Petrom. This commitment is a guiding principle for handling human rights issues in daily business activities, encompassing specific obligations like workers' rights, protective measures, vulnerable groups, environmental and climate change effects. The policy also details the company's due diligence process, grievance management, awareness, and communication strategies. The Policy Statement is applicable across the OMV Petrom Group, its consolidated subsidiaries and its supply chain and it is accessible through the company's internal regulatory platform and our corporate website.

[MDR-P 65b-f] For more information, please see Climate change chapter.

[S3-1.16a] OMV Petrom is committed to equality, irrespective of factors such as race, gender, sexual identity, age, colour, ethnic or social origin, genetic features, language, religion or belief, political or any other opinion, membership of a national minority, indigenous origin, property, family status, disability, health status (including mental health), or any other status.

[S3-1.16b] The company maintains regular dialogues with legitimate representatives from local communities with whom OMV Petrom interacts and whom the company regards as crucial partners in achieving its objectives.

[S3-1.16c] OMV Petrom aims to provide remedy to correct negative impacts on the affected communities. Recognizing grievance mechanisms as vital tools for identifying and managing negative impacts on local communities, employees, and other stakeholders, the company ensures that its grievance mechanism addresses various categories of impacts including human rights. OMV Petrom aims to resolve all grievances in accordance with the UN Effectiveness Criteria.

[S3-1.17] OMV Petrom respects the Universal Declaration of Human Rights and international treaties like the International Labor Organization (ILO) core treaties (e.g., Freedom of Association and Protection of the Right to Organise Convention No. 87, Right to Organise and Collective Bargaining Convention No. 98, Forced Labour Convention No. 29, Abolition of Forced Labour Convention No. 105, Minimum Age Convention No. 138, Worst Forms of Child Labour Convention No. 182, Equal Remuneration Convention No. 100, Discrimination -Employment and Occupation- Convention, No. 111). Having signed the UN Global Compact, the company is fully committed to the UN Guiding Principles on Business and Human Rights and the OECD Guidelines for Multinational Enterprises.

The company is committed to upholding salient human rights and expects its employees and business partners to adhere to the same principles. Based on audits performed, no noncompliance cases were identified.

[S3-1.AR9] There have been no changes to the policies pertaining to affected communities, or to OMV Petrom's approach to engagement, due diligence and remedy processes compared to the previous year, with the exception of the Social Investments Standard which was adopted in
<!-- Report Page 187 -->
2024. The changes were necessary to increase integration
of our social involvement projects and to better respond to communities' needs. The contents of this standard are described in the beginning of the Policies section of this chapter.

## [S3-2] Engaging with affected communities

[S3-2.21a] OMV Petrom engages directly with various stakeholders, including authorities, members of the public, or local NGOs from the communities where it operates. Recognizing these community members as vital partners in achieving its objectives, the company maintains regular dialogues to ensure their voices and concerns are heard and addressed. We conducted consultation with potentially affected communities in all our E&P Assets during various phases of the business projects (e.g. submitting the request for issuing the environmental permit). Another example is the consultation procedures for the Environmental Permit of our Neptun Deep project, which took place in Tuzla, Romania, and Varna, Bulgaria, in early 2024.

[S3-2.21b] The engagement continues throughout the project lifecycle - during commissioning, operational phases, decommissioning, and even abandonment - to guarantee that community viewpoints are consistently considered and addressed.

The community and social investments of OMV Petrom are informed by the needs identified through ongoing community engagement processes. Annually, the company prioritizes collaborative projects with local stakeholders, aiming to optimize the social return on its investments.

[S3-2.21c] At OMV Petrom, the responsibility for ensuring engagement with local communities and stakeholders is entrusted to the business divisions. The Sustainability corporate function oversees the implementation of community relations and development plans, as well as ensuring regular collection and review of stakeholder reports and feedback, and monitors the implementation of Group guidelines, policies, and regulations. The highest function responsible for the engagement are Business Heads, this can be General Managers, VPs or Asset Managers.

[S3-2.21d] Regular, organized alignment sessions are conducted with community development professionals to oversee and guide the local implementation of site-specific community relations and development strategies, plans and projects. Effectiveness is ensured through the collection and review of reports and feedback on the implemented projects, which further informs the company's community development plans.

[S3-2.22] OMV Petrom uses several methods to identify and understand the perspectives of communities whose interests or rights are affected by, or who can influence, the company's development and operational activities, including particularly vulnerable and marginalized groups.

To gain insight into the perspectives of these vulnerable groups within targeted communities, such as women, children, people with special needs, minorities, and migrants OMV Petrom takes the following steps:

Stakeholder Dialogue to understand their concerns and needs;

Collaboration with NGOs through various social projects, sponsorships, and donations to gain further valuable insights into the specific vulnerabilities and perspectives of these groups. Project reports received from implementing NGOs include feedback from beneficiaries;

Grievance Mechanisms to ensure that the concerns of all community members, including those of vulnerable groups, are heard and addressed effectively.

For information regarding how the company gains insight into the perspectives of vulnerable communities, please refer to the Our approach to community relations section of this chapter.

<!-- Report Page 188 -->
## [S3-3] Remediation and channels to raise concerns

[S3-3.27a] OMV Petrom aims to provide remedy through its Grievance Mechanism, which is integrated into the Compliance Management System. This approach is meant to enhance the efficiency and effectiveness of dialogue with stakeholders and communities, enabling remedies where needed and reducing non-compliance risks and financial consequences for the company. The Grievance Mechanism is designed to be transparent, credible, and equitable, fostering trust and advancing OMV Petrom's social performance in community relations.

The Grievance Mechanism process is described below:

Receiving and registering the grievances

Managing the grievances:

Identify the requirements;

Review the grievance;

Register the grievance in the database;

Document the grievance, based on the documents received and with the support of the existing databases in the company;

Field visits, if necessary, in order to clarify the issues;

Prepare the Field findings Protocol or the Protocol for granting compensations for the damages caused, if the case. If, following the findings in the field, the petitioner gives up his requests, it is mandatory to stipulate this information in the Protocol, under the signature of both parties in two copies with one copy to the petitioner;

Distribute to other Departments, with the notification of the Organizational Unit Manager, to obtain necessary information / documents as to formulate the response;

Request additional information and / or documents from the petitioner;

Propose the response to the petitioner and the corrective measures, together with the activity responsibles in whose competence the issues reported by the petitioner fall;

Formulate the response to the petitioner, secure the signature and sent to petitioner, in accordance with the applicable internal rules;

Register in the database of the response and supporting documents, if any;

Follow-up of the implementation of corrective measures by the activity responsible in whose competence the issues reported by the petitioner fall;

Reporting on grievance status and resolution (through reports obtained from the database weekly, monthly, annually).

The effectiveness of the provided remedies is assessed by evaluating the resolution of issues and ensuring timely communication of outcomes to the concerned parties.

[S3-3.27b] OMV Petrom receives grievances from affected communities which can be submitted personally by the petitioner or his representative; by courier or standards/registered post; by fax; by e-mail; by phone, at company's toll free phone numbers; by any company's social media platforms. The Petrobrazi Refinery operates its dedicated channel, where any community member can address any question or complaint by phone or by email. This channel is promoted regularly, especially around periods of major activity within the refinery.

[S3-3.27c] OMV Petrom Group ensures the availability of grievance channels both within its direct operations and through its business relationships. This is achieved by including the management of grievances as a contractual requirement for partners, suppliers, and contractors.

[S3-3.27d] OMV Petrom employs a systematic approach to tracking and monitoring issues raised through its grievance mechanisms. The company maintains a detailed record of all grievances, which allows for:

<!-- Report Page 189 -->
Registering the number of grievances by category

Measuring the number of received grievances versus the number of resolved grievances

Measuring the number of grievances resolved through remediation

In 2024, we registered a total of 602 grievances related to society, human rights and environmental issues, 374 of the grievances were related to social impacts generated by the operations and 228 grievances were related to environmental impacts. 522 were resolved, out of which 203 through remediation.

Each grievance is thoroughly investigated and addressed in a non-judicial manner, ensuring that community members have access to appropriate remedies tailored to the specifics of their case. The Compliance Department conducts regular audits of the grievance database to verify the accuracy of the data and to make recommendations for enhancements where necessary. This monitoring is essential to ensure that grievances are resolved in a timely and effective manner.

Additionally, OMV Petrom assesses the effectiveness of its grievance mechanism by evaluating the resolution outcomes and the satisfaction of the stakeholders involved. This evaluation helps the company to continuously improve its processes and ensure that its channels remain accessible,

responsive, and effective in addressing the concerns of the communities it engages with.

[S3-3.28] To ensure that affected communities are not only aware of OMV Petrom's grievance processes but also trust in their effectiveness, the company monitors the number and types of grievances submitted. By analyzing the ratio of received grievances to resolved grievances, OMV Petrom gains insights into the reliability of its process. Additionally, tracking the number of resolved grievances serves as an indicator of the process's effectiveness in providing remediation. This close monitoring of grievances is communicated to the stakeholders involved, ensuring transparency and accountability.

[S3-3.28] In accordance with the EU Whistleblowing Directive and its relevant topics, including but not limited to corruption and bribery, conflicts of interest, competition law, and capital markets law, OMV Petrom has developed an internal Whistleblowing Directive. This directive stipulates a "no retaliation" policy aimed at protecting individuals who report misconduct in good faith and outlines methods for stakeholders, including members of affected communities, to make confidential and anonymous whistleblower complaints. The Whistleblowing Directive also ensures that reports or incidents are investigated by Compliance Department.

Additionally, OMV Petrom's Grievances Management Standard emphasizes that information, including reasonable suspicions, regarding actual or potential violations of the law, which have occurred or are likely to occur within the company and are reported internally or externally (through written or oral communication), shall be treated in accordance with the provisions of Law 361/2022 on the protection of whistleblowers in the public interest.

## [S3-4] Actions

[S3-4-31, S3-4-38] OMV Petrom is dedicated to addressing the needs of all local communities in which it operates, strategically allocating actions and resources to ensure that over 80% of these communities benefit from its social responsibility programs. In 2024, OMV Petrom invested about RON 131 mn in such initiatives.

OMV Petrom initiates actions to prevent and mitigate negative impacts and advance positive impacts on affected communities. The specific actions linked to the actual positive impact of creating opportunities for communities around suppliers and own operations and for mitigating the potential negative impact related to community dependence on OMV Petrom are part of the company's Social Investments Annual Plan (which includes working with local

and national NGOs) and target three key areas: education, health and environment. The coverage of these actions spans across OMV Petrom's own operations, and they are annual.

## Education

The "Start in education" program, coordinated annually by the OMV Petrom Foundation and World Vision Romania, which aims to offer educational support to disadvantaged communities, reached 60,000 children (3-6 years old), 11,200 parents, 3,700 educators and 1,500 kindergartens with a total amount of RON 21 mn. The interventions were implemented in over 2,290 disadvantaged communities in 41 counties, having a nation-wide scope. Through the " Let's be friends - No bullying " program, the OMV Petrom Foundation and Save the Children Romania contributed to the psychoemotional development of 8,800 children aged 3 to 6 from 79 kindergartens in 8 counties and Bucharest. Also, over 600 teachers acquired the necessary skills to implement the Free of bullying methodology in the classroom, and over 8,800 parents were informed about positive educational methods. The investment in this project was of 841,000 RON.

The "Prahova Education Fund", coordinated annually by the Prahova Community Foundation, supported 12 community projects (through digital infrastructure, STEAM or sports activities and which aim to develop the skills of children, young people, adults from vulnerable groups) with a total amount of RON 2 mn. The action cover the Prahova county.

## Health

Access to medical care is an important basic need that OMV Petrom aims to support, covering local rural communities in Romania. The "Sănătate în vecinătate" program, run by the Medcorp Association and supported by OMV Petrom with RON 8 mn in 2024, continued to provide medical services for over 5,000 beneficiaries.

In 2024, OMV Petrom invested RON 8 mn to continue the partnership with HOSPICE Casa Speranței Foundation. Over 500 children and 1,000 relatives will benefit in 2025 from the palliative care services offered by the foundation. The care services covered beneficiaries across Romania.

OMV Petrom Foundation coordinated projects that address neonatal and prenatal care, ensuring better care and a healthier start for the next generation. The "Baby Box" program implemented together with Save the Children Romania and SAMAS spans 1,300 nation-wide communities, with a significant investment of RON 20 mn in

2024, providing critical support to more than 15,300 mothers and newborns. Through the "First Step to Health" program, the OMV Petrom Foundation and Save the Children Romania expanded access to medical services for 22,000 pregnant women, mothers and children nationwide and equipped another 60 general practitioners' facilities with medical equipment with a total investment of RON 5 mn in
<!-- Report Page 190 -->
2024. Through the "Together from the first day" program, the
OMV Petrom Foundation and Save the Children Romania equipped 16 additional medical units reaching a total of 60 medical units with state-of-the-art medical equipment for monitoring the health of newborns with a total investment of RON 8 mn in 2024.

## Environment

In 2024, OMV Petrom and Energy Policy Group (EPG) announced the completion of the third educational unit in the country upgraded to the nZEB standard - nearly zero-emission building, as part of the "România Eficientă" program. The budget for upgrading the building amounted to RON 7,5 mn.

Through the "Romania plants for tomorrow" annual program OMV Petrom planted 11 urban and rural forests. The company invested mn 16 RON to plant 600,000 saplings across the whole country with the help of Mai Mult Verde, Act for Tomorrow, Asociația Parcul Natural Văcărești, Pădurea Copiilor, Plantăm Fapte Bune în România and Adoptă un Copac.

Through "The Green for the Future" program the OMV Petrom Foundation financed the Vânători Neamț Natural Park (PNVNT) with RON 4 mn in order to become a national role model for biodiversity conservation and the development of the ecotourism destination concept.

In order to mitigate the potential negative impact related to noise and disturbance of surrounding communities, OMV Petrom has undertaken the following specific actions:

Continuous optimization of routes in order to deliver the company's products in the most efficient way [MDR-A-a]. This action covers communities surrounding OMV Petrom's operations and across its value chain [MDR-Ab], and is undertaken continuously [MDR-A-c].

Clauses related to fleet age with direct impact in minimization of emissions and additional requirements for tyre management processes to ensure road safety and reduced noise emissions [MDR-A-a]. This action covers communities surrounding OMV Petrom's operations [MDR-A-b], and is undertaken annually [MDRA-c].

[S3-4-32a] For information regarding the actions undertaken by OMV Petrom to prevent the potential negative impact related to process safety incidents, please refer to the Pollution and Process safety chapters.

[S3-4-32c] OMV Petrom's actions and initiatives in place to deliver positive impacts for affected communities are reported above in the "Key actions" section of this chapter.

[S3-4-32d] To evaluate the effectiveness of its actions and initiatives, OMV Petrom utilizes a range of quantitative metrics, including the total investment amount, the number of projects implemented, and the number of beneficiaries reached. This comprehensive system provides a clear picture of the scope and reach of the company's initiatives. It is designed to measure both the immediate outcomes and the long-term impact of OMV Petrom's social investments.

[S3-4-33a][S3-4-33b][S3-4-33c] OMV Petrom's uses its grievance mechanism as the main process to identify what action is needed in response to negative impacts, as well as for providing and enabling remedy.

For more information regarding the grievance mechanism, please refer to the "Remediation and channels to raise concerns" section of this report.

[S3-4-34] OMV Petrom has not identified material risks and opportunities arising from impacts and dependencies on affected communities, in line with its double materiality process.

<!-- Report Page 191 -->
[S3-4-35] OMV Petrom is dedicated to ensuring that its practices do not cause or contribute to material negative impacts on affected communities. To achieve this, the company engages in ongoing dialogue with local communities to identify, mitigate, or prevent impacts and other repercussions that may arise from its operations.

OMV Petrom has established an internal regulation for conducting Environmental and Social Impact Assessments (ESIAs) for its business projects. This regulation allows the company to understand the types of impacts its operations may cause and the mitigation measures that need to be implemented. Public consultations, which are part of the EIA process, ensure that the perspectives and concerns of affected communities and stakeholders are incorporated into the assessments.

For information regarding the actions undertaken by OMV Petrom to prevent the potential negative impact related to process safety incidents, please refer to the Pollution and Process safety chapters.

[S3-4-36] OMV Petrom monitors the number of severe human rights issues and incidents connected to affected communities in our organization or in our value chain. During 2024, the company has identified 0 such cases.

### Metrics and targets [DR S3-5] Targets

[S3-5-39] Maintain >80% of local communities covered by yearly social investments

[MDR-T-80a, b, c, d, e, f, i, j] OMV Petrom's target to maintain >80% of local communities covered by yearly social investments refers to the company's own operations and entails 41 out of 41 Romanian counties covered each year. The target aims to advance the company's positive impact related to the creation of opportunities for communities. No baseline was set, as the target applies yearly. There have been no changes to the target or the underlying measurement methodologies, significant assumptions, limitations, sources and processes to collect data adopted within the defined time horizon. We continuously monitor the progress and report it annually. In 2024 we have reached 100% of local communities.

Yearly spend with local suppliers to be at least 85% of total Procurement spend

[MDR-T-80a, b, c, d, e, f, h, i, j] OMV Petrom aims to support local communities in areas where the company operates by increasing local procurement and aligns with the procurement expectations of neighbouring communities, while also reducing business disruptions. The target is absolute and measured as a percentage of total procurement spend. It applies to the upstream value chain across the entire OMV Petrom Group. No baseline was set, as the target applies yearly. Internal stakeholders and external benchmarks were involved in setting this target. No changes in the target and corresponding metrics or underlying measurement methodologies, significant assumptions, limitations, sources and processes to collect data have been undertaken during the reporting period. OMV Petrom's target regarding yearly spend with the local suppliers is monitored on an ongoing basis through an own digital tool and it is annually reported. In 2024 we have reached 85,1% out of total Procurement spend.

With regards to the potential negative impact related to process safety incidents, a target is set to Maintain the leading position in the industry in Process Safety Event Rate, which also covers the negative impact Critical incidents related to the oil and gas sector include loss of control or containment of hydrocarbons, well blowout,

explosions, fires, unplanned plant disruption and shutdown pertaining to the entity-specific topic Process Safety. Please refer to the Process safety chapter, for more information regarding this target.

[S3-5-42a, S3-5-42c] OMV Petrom's process for setting targets and identifying lessons for improvement is informed by multiple internal stakeholders and their expectations in

relation to the company. However, affected communities have not been directly involved in this process.

[S3-5-42b] The company communicates its performance against these targets on an annual basis through its sustainability statement, which is publicly accessible on the corporate website, as part of the Annual Report.
